### E2E flake fixed — "Saved" confirmation cut short by the post-save reload (2026-09-28)
Closes the `[NOTE]` above: both "authoring an interactive stage" tests intermittently timed out waiting for "Saved" after clicking "Save stage", with no error and the fields filled. The suspected race (test fills/clicks before the Course/Stage-change draft load lands) was ruled out: Save is disabled and the editor fields don't render until the matching draft loads, and Playwright's click waits for enabled. The failure snapshot pointed elsewhere — the badge read **Authored** and **Remove stage** was shown, so the save had persisted and a *later* load had completed.
- `[FIX]` **`StructureAuthor` no longer reloads the draft after a successful save.** `onSave` bumped `reloadKey`, re-running the load effect, which ends in `setStatus("idle")` — clearing the 2.5 s "Saved" confirmation as soon as `getStructure` returned. Measured on the production build it was on screen for **19–34 ms** (12/12 samples): imperceptible to an editor, and seen by `toBeVisible` only when a poll happened to land inside it (Playwright's backoff settles at 500 ms). The reload also overwrote anything typed while it was in flight. `saveStructure` stores the draft verbatim, so the component keeps its draft and only marks the saved (course, stage) as authored — guarded on the key so switching selectors mid-save can't mislabel another stage. `router.refresh()` stays; "Remove stage" still reloads (the emptied draft is its intended end state).
- `[VERIFY]` "Saved" now stays up **~2.5 s** (12/12). The describe passed **25/25 runs (50/50 tests)** vs **1/12 runs failing** on the unchanged build — no test change needed. tsc ✓ eslint ✓ **74 unit ✓**, production build ✓, **49/49 E2E ✓** on a real local Postgres, secret scan 0.

### Invitations: "sent" now means sent — and failures say why (2026-09-29)
Reported: an admin added a learner, but the invitation email never arrived.
- `[FIX]` **Root cause (SENIC's code): refused invitations were reported as sent.** With `redirect: false`, next-auth's `signIn()` does not throw when sending fails — Auth.js catches the error, logs it, and returns its error-page URL (`…/api/auth/error?error=Configuration`). `sendInvitation` only watched for exceptions, so a refused email showed *"sent them an invitation email"*, was audited as `member.invited`, and listed as *Invited <date>*. **Reproduced on the then-live code** (Resend refusing with 401 while the screen said sent). Now `lib/signin-result.ts` (unit-tested) counts an invitation as sent only when Auth.js reaches the "check your email" step (`/verify-request`).
- `[FIX]` **Admins are told why.** The invite provider records Resend's own error message on the invitation intent (Auth.js swallows it); the People screen shows *"couldn't be sent. The email service said: "…"."*; nothing is audited as invited on failure.
- `[BUILD]` **Every send is logged** (sign-in + invitation): `[email] Resend accepted the … email to …@domain (id …)` — the id matches Resend's dashboard — or `[email] Resend refused … — name (status) — reason`. Recipient **domain only**, never the full address.
- `[SECURITY]` On the live site (`VERCEL_ENV=production`) a missing `RESEND_API_KEY` now fails loudly instead of the dev fallback, which would have silently sent nothing **and printed working sign-in links into the server logs**. Local dev / CI / previews keep the console fallback.
- `[VERIFY]` Evidence gathered: production sends email (sign-in link to Resend's test inbox `delivered@resend.dev` → accepted); the **real key + `senic.world` sender deliver an invitation** (Resend accepted, id `01a0ec42…`); with Resend refusing (invalid key, nothing sent) the admin now sees the reason, no audit, *Not joined yet*. tsc ✓ eslint ✓ **88 unit** ✓ (+5) · production build ✓ · **full E2E 55/55** on a fresh local Postgres. Troubleshooting steps added to GO-LIVE §4.
- `[NOTE]` Which case hit the reported learner can't be told from code: after deploy, **Resend invitation** on that person gives the true answer; if it says sent, Resend's dashboard (Emails) shows Delivered / Bounced — then check spam or the address.

### Round 1 of the 8-point change list — uploads, Admin = Editor, no "demo", logo, learner formatting (2026-09-29)
Reported with screenshots: video upload said *"Preview only — sign in as a content editor (and connect Vercel Blob)"* for an admin although the Blob store shows *Connected*; demo/Strapi wording on the working platform; no logo; learners saw a paragraph with `•` bullets as one wall of text. Built as four parallel workstreams in isolated git worktrees (`r1-uploads`, `r1-no-demo`, `r1-brand`, `r1-learner-text`), each independently reviewed and fixed by a second engineer before merging.
- `[FIX]` **Uploads — root cause:** the live deployment has **no Blob token** (probe: `POST /api/video/upload` → *"Vercel Blob: No read-write token found"*), although the store is connected — env vars only reach deployments built after they exist. The UI swallowed every error and printed a hard-coded guess. Now: `lib/blob-token.ts` finds the token under the standard name **or any prefixed `…READ_WRITE_TOKEN`** (value must be a `vercel_blob_rw_` token) and passes it explicitly to `handleUpload`; routes answer stable codes (`storage-missing` 503, `signed-out` 401, `not-editor` 403, `rate-limited` 429).
- `[BUILD]` **Upload UX:** `GET /api/storage/status` (signed-in only; never returns a value or variable name) drives a line under every picker — *File storage connected* or the one real blocker (not signed in / no Editor role / storage not connected + what an admin does). Pre-checks size/type before sending; files > 50 MB go multipart; progress *"Uploading… 48.2 of 126.3 MB · about 2 min left"*; leave-page warning mid-upload; **Try again**; the browser SDK hides the server's refusal text, so failures re-ask the status endpoint instead of guessing. A local preview is labelled *Not saved yet* and can no longer be saved to a lesson (learners would have got a broken video).
- `[FIX]` **Admin includes Editor (D28):** one rule in `lib/permissions.ts` used by every authoring action, both routes, the workspaces and draft preview. All *"sign in as a content editor"* texts are gone; save failures say what actually happened. Manage people shows *Admin (includes Editor)*.
- `[FIX]` **No demo wording (D30):** removed the "platform shell — a working demo" banners, the Strapi badge/note, the dummy project picker, the fake *Live preview* page (it faked its save; `/content/preview` now redirects), *Content models*, the made-up localisation tree, *Editorial workflow*, the made-up audit log and *"anonymised by default"* claim (false: staff views show names), the *"You (demo learner)"* mock. The live Statistics page shows real data or says there is none (sample data only on test copies). **`/demo` and `/dev/*` 404 on the live site.** A unit test fails the build if *demo / Strapi / "in production this"* reappears in user-facing text.
- `[SECURITY]` **Staff-only pages decide before loading data:** `/content` rendered for anyone (authoring tools for learners/guests; the course picker sent **unpublished draft titles** to whoever opened the URL); `/admin` showed live counts to signed-out visitors. Both now show *Editors only* / *Admins only* (shared `AccessNotice`).
- `[BUILD]` **Official logo (D32):** assets generated reproducibly from the supplied PNG by `scripts/brand-assets.py` (lockup, mark, email tile on white, favicon.ico/icon.png/apple-icon.png, manifest 192/512 + maskable, Open Graph/Twitter 1200×630). Header (mark + wordmark), production landing, sign-in, check-email, test sign-in, and both emails (absolute URL from the link's own origin, alt text kept). Site metadata moved to the root layout so link previews carry the image. Boilerplate `public/*.svg` removed.
- `[FIX]` **Learner text keeps its formatting:** `lib/rich-text/legacy.ts` (pure, 32 tests) turns authored plain text into paragraphs with line breaks, bullet lists (•, -, –, *, Word's `·/o/§` + tab levels, inline "a • b • c" runs) and numbered lists (keeps the start number); `FormattedText` renders them as real `<p>/<ul>/<ol>` with a shared `.seq-prose` style — no HTML, no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`. Used for narrative paragraphs, callouts, compare sides, reflection intros, simulation situations and scenario setups/outcomes. Every bundled string still renders exactly as before.
- `[FIX]` **Server functions pinned to Frankfurt (D31):** the live site ran functions in `iad1` (US East, `x-vercel-id: cdg1::iad1`) while Neon and Blob are in Frankfurt — every query crossed the Atlantic and personal data was processed in the US. `vercel.json` → `regions: ["fra1"]`.
- `[FIX]` Smaller: the sign-in email escapes its link; `/dev` also 404s when test sign-in is off (covers self-hosting).
- `[VERIFY]` tsc ✓ eslint ✓ **209 unit** ✓ (+121) · production build ✓ · **full E2E 56/56** on a fresh local Postgres (`seqelevate_r1`).
- `[NOTE]` The machine's disk was full (≈200 MB free, OrbStack stopping itself, Playwright's browser purged by macOS). Cleared npm's download cache (`npm cache clean --force`, 4.3 GB, regenerates on demand); reinstalled Playwright's headless Chromium. Another project's Supabase containers, auto-started with OrbStack, were stopped again.

### Round 2 — "Manage courses": every course on its own page (2026-09-29)
Owner's request: a sixth hub card **Manage courses** → courses as cards → one page per course with its title, subtitle and content blocks (paragraphs, callouts, lists …), lesson narrative, interactive video etc. — replacing "pick a course inside each tool"; catalogue links into each course. Built from a written technical design (read-only mapping of five subsystems first), as a foundation branch (data layer, contracts, messages, workspace wiring) plus three parallel worktrees (course grid · course page · player preview + media tools), integrated with a full E2E run on its own database, then a three-lens adversarial review (14 confirmed findings, all fixed).
- `[BUILD]` **Hub:** sixth card *Manage courses* (Admins + Editors), 3×2 grid: Create courses · Manage courses · Manage people · Support learners · Statistics · My learning. The header switcher names the page correctly (`workspaceForPath` now picks the longest matching route).
- `[BUILD]` **Course grid** `/[locale]/content/courses`: one card per course — status (Published / Draft / Archived, in words), Built-in / Created here, language chips, lesson count, last edited, enrolled; *Open course*, *Preview as learner*, and a numbered link straight to each lesson. A *New course* card first.
- `[BUILD]` **Course page** `/[locale]/content/courses/<slug>` (deep links `?lesson=`, `&lang=`): title + subtitle per language (built-in courses too — new nullable column `Course.copyOverride`, *Use built-in wording* to undo), one content-language switch for the whole page, publishing (Publish / Unpublish with confirm / Archive / Move back to draft, readiness warnings), a plain *"This course is live — changes you save are visible to learners straight away"* note, lessons in learner order — each shows what learners see and opens to edit: interactive video (upload/replace/remove, captions, questions), documents (draft/visible, clearly labelled), the lesson text as **content blocks** (move, duplicate, add below, delete with undo, and an *Add block* template picker: Paragraph, Key message, Numbered list, Compare two sides), and the interactive stages on created courses. Built-in reflection/interactive stages are read-only with an explanation (D33).
- `[BUILD]` **No course pickers inside tools any more:** video, documents, interactive stages and lesson text take their lesson from the course page. *Create courses* now only starts a new course (then opens its page) and holds the shared Comp Card template. Unused public server actions removed (`getCatalogue`, `listAuthorableCourses`, `getLessonContent`).
- `[BUILD]` **Preview as learner is a sandbox (D35):** `?preview=1` (and every draft an editor opens) plays the real course but writes nothing — no enrolment, telemetry, badge or audit — can start at any lesson, and shows **Edit this lesson** / **Back to editing** in the editor's own menu language.
- `[SECURITY]` Every new page and loader checks the Editor role on the server before loading anything; `updateCourseMeta` validates types, locales and lengths, locks the row, audits `course.meta_updated`, and copies the current status when it first creates a built-in course's row (rewording never unpublishes a live course). Lesson-text and interactive-stage saves are now transactional with a version check: a save from an out-of-date page is refused ("Someone else saved this since you opened it") instead of silently overwriting a colleague's translation.
- `[FIX]` Found on the way: the signed-in header scrolled sideways on phones (up to 97 px at 360 px) — labels now appear progressively by width, the sign-out button kept an accessible name; the header language switch no longer drops `?preview` / `?lesson`; unsaved edits are guarded on in-app navigation too; focus and announcements after publish/remove actions; lesson headers no longer squeeze their title on phones (caught in a manual check at 375 px).
- `[VERIFY]` tsc ✓ eslint ✓ **331 unit** ✓ (incl. messages-parity: EN/DE/EL have identical keys and placeholders) · production build ✓ · **full E2E 77/77** on a fresh local Postgres (`seqelevate_r2m`), incl. new `course-grid`, `course-page`, `player-preview` specs with axe scans and 360 px checks. Manual walk-through in the browser at 1280 px and 375 px.
- `[NOTE]` Migration `20260929120000_course_copy_override` (additive, nullable) runs on the Vercel build via `migrate-deploy`. Known limits: the browser Back button isn't guarded against unsaved edits; dashboard progress is still counted out of 7 lessons for created courses (older issue).

### Round 3 — rich text: bold, italic, bullets, numbering, indent — and authors see what learners see (2026-09-29)
Owner's request: paragraph text editable with bold, italic, bullet points, numbering and indent, formatted exactly as learners see it. Built from the written design as a foundation branch (format, sanitizer, save path) plus two parallel worktrees (learner renderer · editor), integrated with a full E2E run and a bundle check, then a three-lens adversarial review (7 confirmed findings, all fixed).
- `[BUILD]` **Editor** (Tiptap 3.31.3, MIT, pinned; explicit extension list — nothing beyond what learners can see): paragraphs and key messages get a toolbar — **Bold, Italic, Bulleted list, Numbered list, Indent, Outdent, Undo, Redo** — with ⌘/Ctrl+B/I, Tab/Shift-Tab in lists, ⌘/Ctrl+] / [ for indent (they never fall through to the browser's Back/Forward). Toolbar is one Tab stop with arrow-key navigation and pressed states; Tab at the deepest list level leaves the editor (no keyboard trap). Loaded only on authoring pages (`next/dynamic`, `ssr:false`) and an ESLint rule keeps it out of learner code — verified: exactly one chunk contains ProseMirror, and no learner route references it.
- `[BUILD]` **Paste from Word / Google Docs** with ⌘V/Ctrl+V keeps bold, italic and (nested) lists and tidies everything else away; a tip under the toolbar says so. No separate "Paste from Word" button (D36, owner decision). Plain-text paste follows the same rules as learner text (•, -, 1. …).
- `[BUILD]` **Same look for authors and learners:** one whitelist renderer (`RichTextView`, no HTML injection, re-sanitises on every render) draws the learner view, the course page's read view and the editor preview, with the same `seq-prose` styles as the editing surface. Round 1's `FormattedText` now goes through it — its tests pass **unchanged** and a parity test pins the old markup byte for byte. Blank lines and extra spaces, which learners never see, are flagged in the editor instead of silently differing.
- `[BUILD]` **List blocks** get a Bullets | Numbers choice; the block templates now offer *Bulleted list* and *Numbered list*. Lists saved before keep their numbers.
- `[SECURITY]` **Stored format** `rich = { v: 1, doc }` next to the plain `text`, which the **server** always derives (D36). The save action rebuilds every block from a whitelist (no links, images, styles, headings or unknown nodes), strips control/bidi characters, and enforces limits (100 blocks, 10 000 characters, 1 500 nodes, lists 3 deep, indent 0–4) with author-readable messages naming the block; Round 2's lock + version check is kept; `narrative.saved` / `narrative.cleared` are audited. Review fix: the save path used to scan shared huge strings without a total budget (a ~107 KB request could block the server ~90 s) — now bounded.
- `[FIX]` Untouched old paragraphs stay plain text until an author actually edits them. A malformed block from the CMS or a direct DB write can no longer crash a learner's lesson.
- `[VERIFY]` tsc ✓ eslint ✓ **654 unit** ✓ · production build ✓ · **full E2E 87/87** on a fresh local Postgres (`seqelevate_r3m`), incl. new `rich-text-editor` (format, save, learner view, Word paste, list style, keyboard, blank lines, limits) and `rich-text-learner` (formatting, hostile content stays text, axe, read view = learner view).
- `[NOTE]` Known limits: tables pasted from Word are flattened to paragraphs; Word/Google Docs paste was proven with representative fixtures — worth a check with a few real partner documents. The reflection intro was still a plain text box — made rich text the same day (next entry).

### Reflection intro is rich text too (2026-09-29)
Owner's request: "make the reflection intro rich text too".
- `[BUILD]` On a created course's reflection lesson, the **Introduction** uses the same editor as lesson paragraphs (Bold, Italic, Bullets, Numbering, Indent/Outdent, Undo/Redo, the paste tip), in a new `reflection-intro` variant whose look equals the learner's intro (small, muted text); the visible *Introduction* label names the text box.
- `[BUILD]` Stored as the lesson's single paragraph in the Round 3 format (`rich` + server-derived `text`, D36). `introFromBlocks` (pure, tested) passes the sanitised rich text to the learner only when that paragraph is the intro's source; subtitle/default fallbacks and built-in courses stay plain. The course outline hands the editor the rich text, so opening an intro never drops its formatting.
- `[BUILD]` Learner reflection stage and the course page's read view render it through one `ReflectionIntro` component (the shared `RichTextView`); a plain intro renders byte-identically to before (tested). Untouched plain intros stay plain until edited; Discard / Remove / "Load the saved version" restore the formatted intro.
- `[FIX]` A malformed stored block (e.g. `null`) could make a created course disappear for learners (`getDbCourse` threw); the intro helper now skips non-blocks.
- `[VERIFY]` tsc ✓ eslint ✓ **664 unit** ✓ · production build ✓ (the editor still lives in a single authoring-only chunk) · **full E2E 88/88** twice on a fresh local Postgres, incl. a new spec: format the intro, save, DB has `rich.v === 1`, read view and a real learner see bold + list; saving only the title leaves a plain intro plain. Two-lens adversarial review: no confirmed findings.
- `[NOTE]` Seen once on a cold server (not caused by this change): if a signed-in learner moves on before their saved progress has loaded, that early progress isn't saved (race in `src/store/demo-state.tsx`). Flagged as a separate task.
- `[NOTE]` Disk was down to ~250 MB again; freed ~2 GB with `pnpm store prune` (unreferenced packages only) and by removing this project's local build output.

### Learner progress is never lost or overwritten (2026-09-30)
Owner's request: "fix the learner progress bug too" (flagged in the previous entry). Built repro-first on a branch: a failing end-to-end test for each way progress could be lost, then the fix, then two adversarial review passes, each finding reproduced and fixed the same way.
- `[FIX]` **Progress made before a signed-in learner's data loaded** (on a cold server a learner can walk a whole course first) was dropped or never saved; a badge earned meanwhile was never awarded. The saved and new progress are now merged (`progress-merge.ts`), and a course is saved only once its saved progress has loaded and been merged (D37).
- `[FIX]` **Failed loads** — including a session lookup that fails, which Auth.js answers as "no session" — were taken for "nothing saved", and the page then saved its copy over the learner's. Loads and saves now fail on "no session" and are retried (`retry.ts`, `pending-save.ts`); nothing is saved until a load gets through.
- `[FIX]` **Sessions changing hands:** signing out or into another account in another tab could move a learner's progress into the browser's guest store or the next account; a completion screen could complete the course again for them. The page now holds a learner's data through a session read that fails (`session-owner.ts`), lets go of it when someone else holds the session, remounts the learner pages and has their server-rendered parts rendered again. Each save carries the learner it was made for; the server refuses it for anyone else.
- `[FIX]` **Guest → account move** (`guest-migration.ts`): each write is retried and one failure no longer stops the rest; the account's saved data wins, also when the sign-in link opens in another tab and this tab signs in without reloading (found in the release check: the guest's Comp Card and answers were saved over the account's).
- `[FIX]` A course opened after another no longer starts at the other course's lesson or its completion screen; opening a course and doing nothing still enrols no one; a save still failing when the learner leaves a course and comes back is sent before the course loads again.
- `[VERIFY]` 24 new end-to-end tests in the learner-data spec (repros that failed on the old code, plus guards), unit tests for every helper.

### Reading-help settings are never reset to defaults (2026-09-30)
Owner's request: "fix the reading-help settings bug too". Same method: 10 new end-to-end tests first (7 failing on the old code; 3 guards passing), fix, two review passes.
- `[FIX]` A failed settings load (or failed session lookup) showed the defaults and then **saved them over the account's own settings**. Now retried, and nothing is saved until it loads (D38).
- `[FIX]` A save on its way when another account signs in on the same browser could land in that account; switching accounts didn't even reload the settings. Saves now carry the account they were made for (`saveA11yPrefs(prefs, forUser)`, refused for anyone else, answering whether it saved); the provider lets go and reloads on a change of hands, and holds through a session blip (`session-hold.ts`).
- `[FIX]` Only settings chosen in this browser that no account holds yet move into an account, and only into one still on the defaults (`a11y-prefs.ts`); a setting chosen while the settings load wins only for itself. Unchanged settings aren't saved.
- `[FIX]` Tests: the reading-help load is recognised by its server action's id from the build manifest (the browser can discard its response body), and the "save still failing" repro now completes a new lesson (a signed-in learner's player opens at lesson 1).

### Release check: every change shows on screen (2026-09-30)
Before releasing the two fixes above: an interplay review of both branches together, a full end-to-end run and repeated runs of the specs they touch, also under CPU load.
- `[FIX]` **The people list sometimes didn't show the person just added** (and the cohort list the cohort just created). Traced to Next.js 16: a `router.refresh()` after an action sometimes doesn't show until the next router update — the server had rendered the new list, the browser didn't commit it. It was already the case on the live site, where the settings save sent after every page load happened to hide it for changes made in the first half-second; the reading-help fix stops sending that needless save. The people and organisations & cohorts actions now return their list and the screens show it at once (D39). Under load: 48/48 and 120/120, where the cohort test failed 11 of 16.
- `[FIX]` **After a sign-out in another tab, the dashboard could keep showing the learner's "Resume" card** for the same reason. The learner layout now says whom it was rendered for; until that matches the session, the page is refreshed again every 2 s and reloaded after the third try. 30/30 under load.
- `[VERIFY]` tsc ✓ eslint ✓ **780 unit** ✓ · production build ✓ · **full E2E 123/123** on a fresh local Postgres (`seqelevate_rel`) · the learner-data, reading-help and invitation specs **three more times: 219/219**.
- `[DECISION]` Tried Next.js 16.2.12 (latest 16.2 patch; upstream reports of refreshes not committing — vercel/next.js#99026, #99028): no change here, so reverted; the workaround doesn't depend on the version (D39).
- `[NOTE]` Known limits: a signed-in learner's player opens a course at its first lesson, not the one they reached (older behaviour; progress is kept); after a sign-out in another tab this tab waits ~3 s before letting go (the session is read again first, D37); a guest who signs in while their own progress is still being read from this browser (a fraction of a second after load) keeps only what was already stored; other screens that rely on a page refresh alone (none known to show stale data in tests) could show the same Next.js stall.
- `[NOTE]` Disk ran out again during the Next.js upgrade attempt (pnpm re-downloaded ~1.8 GB after the earlier store prune); reinstalled `node_modules` from the store (copy-on-write) and removed the finished comparison worktree — ~0.8 GB free afterwards.


### Release 0 — fixes from the partners' change requests (2026-10-13)
Owner's request: "Start Release 0" — the first release of the plan answering the consortium's change-request document (plan §4, due Fri 23 Oct). Built as five parallel branches (player · progress · drafts · accounts · links), each repro-first (a unit test and an end-to-end test that fail on the old code), each reviewed through two lenses and corrected; then merged, the new end-to-end tests run against the **old** build to prove they catch each bug, a cross-cutting five-lens review with an independent check of every finding (10 confirmed, all fixed in four more reviewed branches), and full end-to-end runs.
- `[FIX]` **Quick-check answers are saved** (they never were: the quick check kept them to itself). Saved per question id with the course, for accounts and guests; a learner who comes back finds their answers, score and Continue — and a **Try again** button, as the other practice steps have; only the latest check counts, and that is what the facilitator sees (D49). Answer buttons are lettered A, B, C by position — quick checks made in the editor showed their internal ids ("a0k3f"), read out by screen readers too — and say which answer is chosen (`aria-pressed`).
- `[FIX]` **"What do I do now?"** On guided practice, the applied scenario and the quick check, a short line where Continue will appear says what it waits for (choose an answer; choose what you'd do, then the follow-up; answer every question, then *Check answers*). Each step now **opens at its top**, then takes focus without scrolling (it used to start a smooth scroll and cut it short, leaving learners at the bottom of a tall step); instant with reduced motion.
- `[FIX]` **Progress counts each course's own lessons** — the dashboard bars, "Your journey", the facilitator's per-learner progress and the statistics divided every course by seven, so a finished four-lesson course showed 57% forever (D50).
- `[FIX]` **A translation typed and then cleared shows learners the English**, not a blank prompt, option or heading — field by field, then the first language written; the publishing warnings count a blank-only language as not written, and the lesson editor says whose text it shows (D51).
- `[BUILD]` **Draft saving for exercises.** *Save draft* keeps an unfinished practice, scenario or quick check (new column `Lesson.structureDraft`); learners only ever get what *Save stage* saved. The course page and the publishing warnings mark drafts; the lesson list offers to edit a drafted lesson, not to add it again. *Not ready to save* now names the exact choice, question and box in the editor's language, marks the box and takes the author there. A scenario whose follow-up has no question, choice text or outcome — which trapped learners — is refused by name, as are blank quick-check answers. The editor is translated (German and Greek call it a lesson, as the course page does), including its heading (D52).
- `[SECURITY]` Exercise saves (draft, stage, discard, remove) check the editor role and the course, lock the row and refuse a write over a colleague's newer stage or draft ("changed elsewhere"); text is cleaned and size-limited (titles 200 characters, texts 2,000, 10 options, 8 choices, 6 follow-ups, 20 questions, 8 answers). Stages saved before the limits stay saveable: what is already stored may stay as long as it is, only growth is refused, and a refusal names the box, language and limit. Draft saves and discards are audited.
- `[FIX]` **Staff are no longer turned into learners** on their first page load (everyone got a learner role). A learner role is given only to someone with no role at all. Staff who still hold the old one stay in the database (nothing deleted) but are no longer listed, counted or analysed as learners: the facilitator roster, the admin *Learners* figure, statistics and "learners enrolled" count only people who hold no staff role (D53).
- `[SECURITY]` **Facilitator notes and competence validations** are saved only by a facilitator or admin of the project, about one of its learners, never about a colleague or themselves; notes and evidence over 2,000 characters or of the wrong type are refused. A note that saved while its validation failed isn't written twice when saving again.
- `[FIX]` **Badges on courses created in the platform are saved** — there was no badge row, so the award silently did nothing and the badge was gone after a reload. A course gets its badge when it's created; a migration gives every existing created course one and awards it to everyone who already finished; a new deploy step (`prisma/backfill-course-badges.mjs`, after migrations) does the same on every deploy, and publishing, rewording or finishing a course without a badge gives it one on the spot (D54). An award with no badge row now reports a failure instead of silent success, without holding up sign-in.
- `[BUILD]` **Quick links in lesson text.** Web addresses (https://, http://) and email addresses typed in lesson text, list points, a practice's instruction and a quick check's intro work as links for learners and on the course page: they open in a new tab (`noopener noreferrer`) with a spoken "(opens in a new tab)", are underlined with a solid focus ring, and long addresses wrap on a 320 px phone. Nothing stored changes — they're found when the page is drawn; only http(s) and mailto are ever linked (D55, amends D36).
- `[FIX]` Found by the new checks: in **high contrast**, primary buttons and the current step showed white on the brand lime (1.7:1) — the project colours written on the page beat the high-contrast ones, and the intended olive was itself only 4.4:1; now 7.4:1. At 320 px (WCAG 1.4.10) the **signed-in header** stuck out (2 px for a learner, 7–10 px for staff), a learner row on **Support learners** pushed its page 44 px sideways in German, and a visually hidden table heading escaped the scroll box of **Manage people** (364 px) and **Statistics**; all fit now, in every language (`header-reflow.spec.ts`). A finished learner on the roster was badged "SEQ Elevate" instead of *Completed*. In the **course step bar** the current step's number was white on lime (1.5:1) and, on phones, the steps had no spoken names (a finished step was just "button"); the staff **workspace menu** opened up to 160 px off the left edge of a phone; the language control and the steps' label were in English on German and Greek pages; the guest home page's header stuck out 21 px in German — all fixed (found by a final review of these changes and a 320 px sweep).
- `[FIX]` **Finishing a course is saved at once.** The course's save waits ~0.7 s so quick clicks go together; a learner who left on *Course complete* within that lost the completion. That save is now sent straight away, and whatever save is waiting is sent when the tab is hidden or closed (D37, as amended). Next.js runs server actions one at a time, so it can still wait a moment behind one already on its way (the last step's time); closing the tab in that moment can still lose it — the gap is narrowed, not closed.
- `[VERIFY]` tsc ✓ eslint ✓ **1,127 unit** ✓ (incl. messages-parity) · production build ✓ · every new end-to-end test fails on the old build, each for its bug · **full E2E 166/166** on a fresh local Postgres (`seq_r0g`), no server errors · the Release 0, learner-data, reading-help, accessibility, preview and journey specs twice under CPU load: **254/254** · the learner-data spec three more times on one worker: 171/171 (on a much harsher run — the whole suite ×3 on three workers — three of its timing tests timed out; on one worker they pass) · a sweep of 300 page loads (guest, learner, staff; en/de/el; 320–414 px): no sideways scrolling. The badge migration and the deploy backfill were run against a local database holding pre-release data, twice each: badges, links and awards as intended, nothing changed on the second run.

### Release 1 — the flexible lesson editor (2026-10-13)
Owner's request: "Start Release 1" — plan §3–4: a lesson list of each course's own, building blocks inside any lesson, preview, and a safety catch so courses using what only Release 2's learner player can show are previewed but not published yet. Designed first (four parallel designs, a completeness critique, a final spec and contracts), then built as a foundation (data, rules, server actions, read model; golden and freeze tests) and three parallel UI streams — preview player · lesson list and publishing · lesson content editors — each built repro-first, reviewed through two lenses (correctness; accessibility, wording and translation) and corrected; then merged, integrated, reviewed again across the whole release by four lenses with an independent check of every finding, and verified.
- `[BUILD]` **Lesson list** on every created course's page: add a lesson (blank, text, practice question, branching scenario, quick check, reflection & self-assessment; or a deleted standard lesson back, empty), name lessons per language (up to 40 characters, refused rather than cut), move, hide/show and delete them (the confirmation lists everything that goes). Every change is announced in the page's one live region, keeps focus in a predictable place, and is refused with the reason when a colleague changed the list meanwhile. Up to 15 lessons (D56, D58).
- `[BUILD]` **Building blocks in any lesson**: content added around the lesson's main content — text, practice question, branching scenario, quick check, the lesson's own reflection questions, a 1–5 self-assessment — each moved, removed (in all languages, after a confirmation) and saved like Release 0's exercises: *Save draft*, *Save stage* (checked, naming the box), *Discard*, and refused when a colleague saved first. Added text is written per language. Up to 12 pieces per lesson (D56, D60).
- `[BUILD]` **Preview** of a course built this way in a new sectioned player (the Release 2 learner player in waiting): the course's own lessons, names and order, every kind of content, *Continue* that says what is still unanswered, *Edit this lesson*, and nothing saved — no enrolment, statistics or badge. A course in the standard layout previews in the learner player itself, as before (D60).
- `[BUILD]` **Safety catch**: a created course with a lesson of its own, a hidden, deleted or moved lesson, or added content can't be published until Release 2; *Publish* says why, each reason linking to its lesson, and the server refuses a page that didn't know (audited). On a published course, changes that would add such a reason are refused with the reason; learners' lessons, names and progress stay exactly as in Release 0 (D57, D59).
- `[DECISION]` The main content never moves: two new nullable columns hold the arrangement (`Course.outline`) and the added content (`Lesson.parts`); nothing is copied or backfilled, and an existing course's page, lessons and stored text are unchanged until an editor changes them (D56). The learner path is frozen and pinned by a hash test; a learner walk through a standard created course (en, de) and a built-in course matches a recording made on Release 0, also after every step is renamed (D60). On published courses Release 1 deliberately refuses reorder/hide/new lessons/added content until Release 2 — a deviation from the consortium default, to be confirmed by the owner (D59).
- `[SECURITY]` Every new action checks the editor role and the course's project, takes the Course row lock before any Lesson row, checks the version the page started from, and refuses (never cuts) what is too large. Deleting a lesson learners have is refused while learners are enrolled, whatever the status; *Remove stage* on an exercise learners have likewise. An admin-only repair resets an unreadable arrangement or added content to empty, with what was there in the audit.
- `[FIX]` Found in integration and fixed: the content list had a live region per open lesson (now the page's one, spec 11.3); the first *Save stage* of a new exercise lesson remounted its editor, wiping "Saved" and dropping focus to the page (found by the load run; now the editor stays put); a lesson of the course's own couldn't be deleted while learners were enrolled, so a course reworked with learners could never be published again (now only lessons learners have are protected; review, major); the pre-deploy derivation check failed on a Release 0 database (now reads the not-yet-added columns as empty); lesson names in the content language lacked `lang` on card headings and warnings, interface badges inside content were marked with the content's language, and the added-text editor's buttons, statuses and messages were in English on German and Greek pages (review, minor; its block controls and the server's per-block issue lines stay in English, as in the main lesson editor); after a lesson was deleted from the list with its card open, the card went on showing the deleted exercise (now it starts again from what is stored); *Remove stage* on a lesson of the course's own was refused with learners enrolled, though learners don't get it; and a delete now locks the lesson's row even when it has none, so a colleague's first save waits for it instead of being deleted unseen (second review of the fixes).
- `[VERIFY]` tsc ✓ eslint ✓ **3,003 unit** ✓ (incl. messages-parity, the learner-path freeze and the golden derivation tests) · production build ✓ · **full E2E 203/203** on a fresh local Postgres (`seq_r1f`) — the server logged only the deliberate session failures, the workspace-hub stream error also seen on Release 0, and once a learner-role write for a test user its spec had just deleted · the Release 1, drafts, course-page, preview, rich-text, player and progress specs twice under CPU load on one worker: **156/156** · the learner walk matches the Release 0 recording in English, German and the built-in course, before and after every step is renamed · each integration fix proven by a test that fails on the build before it · the derivation check on 8 Release 0-shaped created courses (stray, draft-only, media-only, over-limit, Greek-only rows): 0 differences, on Release 1's schema and on Release 0's · the deploy scripts (migrations, badge backfill, demo seed) on the new schema, twice; the demo seed leaves an edited demo course alone · a cross-cutting four-lens review (concurrency and guard, security, learner safety and data, UI integration) with an independent check of each finding: 5 confirmed (1 major, 4 minor), all fixed; 2 rejected as by design · a second review of those fixes: 6 found (1 medium, 5 low), all fixed with tests that fail without them.
- `[NOTE]` Migration `20261014120000_course_outline_lesson_parts` (two nullable JSON columns, `IF NOT EXISTS`). The build-seeded demo course is left alone once an editor has added lessons or content to it. `docs/partner-guide-lessons.md` is the input for the 3 Nov walkthrough.
- `[NOTE]` Known limits until Release 2: learners don't see lesson names, lessons of a course's own, added content or a changed order; the preview player re-creates the learner screens' look, so small visual differences are possible; language coverage counts main content only; German and Greek wording (224 new texts) is a first draft for partner review. In the workspace-hub tests, Node logs a stream error ("transformAlgorithm is not a function") on a redirect — it happens on the Release 0 build too, and the tests pass.

### Off-site backups: ready to switch on, and proven on the runner (2026-10-13)
Owner's request: switch on the off-site backups (the nightly job had never run: no secrets). A research workflow (the workflow's first-run risks; read-only Neon access; EU storage providers; client-side encryption), a synthesis and two skeptical checks against official documentation shaped the change.
- `[FIX]` The first real run would have failed: `pg_dump` went through Debian's `pg_wrapper`, which picks the runner's preinstalled PostgreSQL 16 client, and refuses a newer server. The client matching the server's major version is now installed and called by path. AWS CLI 2.23+ default checksums, which break some S3-compatible stores, are off. The runner is pinned to `ubuntu-24.04` (`ubuntu-latest` moves to 26.04 from 19 Oct).
- `[SECURITY]` The backup refuses a database login that can change anything, so the owner's credentials never sit in GitHub by mistake; `scripts/db-backup-role.sql` creates the read-only `backup_reader` role (Neon makes console-created roles full admins, so it is made with SQL). Sign-in session and sign-in-link token rows are left out of dumps. Optional client-side encryption with `age` (only the public key in GitHub; pinned binary checked by SHA-256). The repository is public: logs show sizes and counts, never a secret or host.
- `[BUILD]` Each dump is verified (size, table count, every data block readable; optionally a full test restore), uploaded, read back and compared by SHA-256; pruning keeps the newest 7 whatever happens. Optional dead-man's-switch ping. Steps in `scripts/db-backup.sh`.
- `[VERIFY]` `db-backup-selftest.yml` runs the script end to end on the real runner image against throwaway PostgreSQL 16 and 17 and a throwaway S3 store (Adobe S3Mock — MinIO's public image is gone): matching `pg_dump` (17.11 against 17), the role SQL sufficient, a writable login and Neon's pooled string refused, tokens absent, verify and test restore, age round trip into a real restore with matching row counts, pruning — green on both. The nightly workflow on `main` with no secrets: green, "NO BACKUP TAKEN".
- `[NOTE]` `BACKUP.md` rewritten as the owner's setup (Scaleway Paris recommended: EU company, encryption at rest, one-project keys by clicking, about €0.03/month; Hetzner has no encryption at rest; US providers need the DPO's view) and a restore procedure that checks the stored copy. Corrected: Neon's point-in-time window is 1 day by default on Launch (not ~7) — set it to 7. Once the provider is chosen, `DATA-PROTECTION.md` and `DPA-TEMPLATE.md` list it as a sub-processor.
- `[NOTE]` (2026-10-19, during the owner's setup) The Neon project is still on the **Free** plan, whose history window is fixed at **6 hours**; Launch defaults to 1 day and allows up to 7, Scale up to 30 (Neon's restore-window docs). The 7-day window therefore follows the move to Launch already planned for the pilot (D24). BACKUP.md (layers, B1), GO-LIVE.md, DATA-PROTECTION.md and DPA-TEMPLATE.md now say so. The nightly off-site backup doesn't depend on the plan.
- `[FIX]` (2026-10-20) The owner set the six secrets (Scaleway bucket and application key, read-only `backup_reader` role on Neon); the first real run passed the checks (connection, read-only role, PostgreSQL 18 server and client) and stopped at the dump: `permission denied for schema neon_auth`. That schema is Neon's own sign-in service (Neon Auth), created by Neon; the app doesn't use it (Auth.js keeps its tables in `public`; no reference in the code), and it holds that service's sessions and signing keys. The dump now leaves it out (`--exclude-schema=neon_auth`); any other schema the role can't read still fails the dump loudly. The self-test seeds an unreadable `neon_auth` schema like Neon's and checks the dump succeeds without it.
- `[VERIFY]` (2026-10-20) **First real backup: green** (run 39399058860). PostgreSQL 18 server, `pg_dump` 18.6, read-only role confirmed; 74,821 bytes, 21 tables with data, every block readable; uploaded to Scaleway Paris, read back and matched by SHA-256; server-side encryption reported AES256 (SSE-ONE); prune: 1 object, nothing due. Self-test now also on PostgreSQL 18 (production's version): green on 16, 17, 18. Repo variables set: `BACKUP_MIN_DUMP_BYTES=37000` (about half the first dump), `BACKUP_TEST_RESTORE=true`. DATA-PROTECTION and GO-LIVE record the start date.
- `[FIX]` (2026-10-20) With the nightly test restore switched on, the next run failed it: the dump contains `CREATE EXTENSION pg_session_jwt`, which Neon installs for its own sign-in service and which plain PostgreSQL on the runner doesn't have — and the failed test restore stopped the upload. The test restore now skips, by name and with a message, only extensions the runner can't install (a restore into Neon includes them); everything else must still restore. A failed test restore no longer stops the verified dump from being stored; the run still fails at the end. The self-test hides a real extension (`pg_trgm`) from the runner to prove the skip, and checks an installable one isn't skipped. BACKUP.md says how to skip it when restoring outside Neon.

### Staff screens: deletes ask first, your own Admin role stays, validations follow the course's skill area (2026-10-20)

- `[FIX]` **Bins ask before deleting.** On People and on Organisations & cohorts, the bin icons deleted at once. They now ask in place (`InlineConfirm`, the markup of the lesson list's delete question): the question takes focus and says what happens, with the number of people in a cohort; Keep or Escape returns focus to the bin. Once Delete or Remove is pressed, Keep is held until the server answers; a failed call says so and can be retried. On a phone the question opens on screen and wraps (WCAG 1.4.10).
- `[FIX]` **Your own Admin role stays on.** `setMemberRoles` refuses switching off your own Admin role; on your own row the Admin chip and the bin are aria-disabled, with the reason. Another admin can still do it. Role chips are 24 px high (WCAG 2.5.8).
- `[FIX]` **Validations start on the course's skill area.** A course created in the platform keeps its skill area in its copy, which the course page can change; the validation's skill area now starts there (`skillClusterOfCourse`), or on none when the copy names none of the seven. The skill area is asked for only with "Validate this evidence", and the screen says that saving again adds a second validation.
- `[FIX]` A cohort's member count counts people, not role rows.
- `[VERIFY]` tsc ✓ eslint ✓ **3,029 unit** ✓ · production build ✓ · **212/212 E2E** on a fresh local Postgres, including `e2e/staff-screens.spec.ts` (each new test failed on the old build first) · GitHub CI on `main` (run 39496616357) ✓ · Vercel production deploy ✓. `docs/staff-guide.md` describes the screens as they now work.

### The session carries only the user's id, email and name (2026-10-21)

- `[FIX]` The session callback answers exactly `{ user: { id, email, name }, expires }`: nothing else of the user or session row that Auth.js passes in, and never roles, which are read from the database on every request. The browser code reads only the user's id and email. Release 3 keeps the same rule (D97).
- `[VERIFY]` `src/auth.test.ts` (4 tests, red before the change) · tsc ✓ eslint ✓ **3,033 unit** ✓ on `main` · production build ✓ · full E2E 178 passed, with `db-backed-progress.spec.ts` (one known flaky dashboard-link timing failure) run again on one worker twice: **114/114** · deployed from `main` (`fa72d3b`).

### Release 2 — flexible courses for learners (2026-10-20)
Owner's request: "Start Release 2" — the third release of the plan answering the consortium's change requests: learners get each course as its editors built it, their answers and progress are kept per lesson and per item, they carry on where they stopped, *Continue* says what it waits for, and a course people are taking can still be changed, with rules that protect their work. Designed first (three designs, a critique, then a final spec with contracts and the owner's defaults), then built as a foundation (data, the merge rules, one lesson plan, the store, the reports) and three parallel streams — learner player · editor rules · dashboards and reports — each built repro-first (a unit test and an end-to-end test that fail on the old code), reviewed through two lenses (correctness; accessibility, wording and translation) and corrected; merged (C, B, A), integrated (the recorded walk's third phase, learners in the middle of a changing course, two finishing at once), reviewed again across the whole release, fixed and verified. Deploy planned for Tue 10 November 2026.
- `[BUILD]` **One learner player for every course** (D63). Learners get a created course as built: its own lessons, its order, its step names in the step bar, *Back* and the counter (now on phones too), hidden lessons and lessons with nothing to show left out, every kind of content (text, practice, scenario, quick check, the lesson's own reflection questions, the 1–5 self-assessment), video and documents. A standard lesson with only its main content keeps its Release 1 screen; everything else is drawn by the sectioned view, so hiding or reordering never changes how a lesson looks, and built-in courses never touch new code. *Preview as learner* is the learner player for every course, with nothing saved. A course with no lesson to show says "This course has no lessons yet." and never completes or gives a badge.
- `[BUILD]` **Answers and progress per lesson and per item** (D62). Two nullable JSON columns on the enrolment (`sectionsCompleted`, `responses`) and one database trigger; the four standard answers stay in their Release 1 columns, so a standard course writes nothing new. The page sends only what changed since the server last confirmed (a patch); the server merges it under the Course and then the enrolment lock, reflection and self-assessment question by question; finished lessons and the completion only ever grow, and the trigger keeps a completion even from an older build. An answer over a limit is refused for that answer alone and the learner is told under the item; a reflection over 5,000 characters says so as it is typed. The Release 1 save action stays for pages loaded before the deploy. Guests get the same within their one course (D69). *Download my data* includes both new columns.
- `[BUILD]` **Resume, order and *Continue*** (D65). A signed-in learner opens at the first lesson they haven't done (the position comes with the page, then one check once their progress loads, which never moves a learner who has started); practice and scenario answers come back on *Back* and on reload; *Try another* works on screen only. The step bar reaches only up to the first lesson not done; *Back* always works; the last *Continue* goes to a lesson still to do and says so. *Continue* is always there, with a hint saying what it waits for; pressed early it names the item and what it needs, marks it, focuses it and scrolls only as far as needed, below the sticky header (instant with reduced motion). Reflection boxes are named by their question, practice options say which is chosen, a done step is read as "…, done", and focus is kept after *Try another* and after a scenario's follow-up. On a phone the in-video question shows below the video.
- `[BUILD]` **Changing a course people are taking** (D66, D67). A course publishes as built; *Publish* refuses only an unreadable lesson list or a course with no lesson to show (audited). With learners, adding, moving, hiding or showing a lesson and adding, moving or removing content ask first, in place, with "N learners are taking this course (M finished)" and what happens to them; the server checks the confirmation and audits it with the counts. Deleting a lesson learners have and removing a question they get are refused (*Hide* offered where it works); *Remove stage* is refused with learners, the course's own lessons included. From 1 December no lesson or content of a course with learners is added, moved or removed, published or not; *Hide* and *Show*, renames and edits stay open. The 15-lesson limit counts hidden lessons; the warning comes when learners would see more than 12. Partner guide v2 (`docs/partner-guide-lessons.md`) says all of this by date.
- `[BUILD]` **Dashboards and reports follow each course's lessons** (D64, D70). One lesson plan counts for the dashboard bars, "Your journey" (the course's step names, and one line for finished lessons no longer shown), the facilitator's *Currently on* (the step name) and time per lesson (the seven standard rows plus *Other lessons*), and the statistics (an *Other lessons* funnel bar, table entry and time column). A finished lesson that is later hidden still counts. The dashboard's main card is the course the learner was last busy in. *Active in the last 24h* now means the last 24 hours (it counted anyone with any progress). The staff guide says what changes on 10 November.
- `[DECISION]` D62–D70 recorded; D57 and D59 are replaced by D66 and D60's freeze is lifted (D63). Settled during the build, each in its decision: the outbox replaces what it held rather than merging (D68); the 1 December rule holds for any course with learners, published or not (D67); a save never creates an enrolment in a course that was never published (D62); under the kill switch a course of its own lessons isn't finished through standard lessons it doesn't have (D63); one look-up in the never-lost tests follows the done step's new name, and those tests recognise the new save body (D63); a waiting save of another course is sent instead of dropped (D62); the learner page's footer passes axe at 320 px (D63); *Active in the last 24h* (D70).
- `[SECURITY]` Learner saves check the session and the learner each save was made for, look the course up in the project, refuse oversized patches whole and oversized answers one by one, never lock a lesson, and are not audited (audit rows outlive erasure). Editor actions keep their role and project checks and take the Course lock before any lesson; confirmations are checked on the server, never trusted from the page. "Course opened", lesson-time and in-video-answer events keep only a platform slug and a true or false. Signing out, or another account signing in, clears the tab's outbox, so no learner's answers stay behind in a shared browser.
- `[FIX]` Found by the reviews and fixed, each with a test that fails without it — Foundation: a stored self-assessment or reflection over today's limits lost its answers when one changed; the outbox could bring old answers back; a guest's course moved into an account lost "the account wins" when it loaded; the list of finished own lessons had no limit; in-video answers kept any course name; the lesson plans loaded every video. Streams: a refused answer was never shown; "added after you started" for a lesson that was only moved; screen readers heard "liveChanges.learners"; a refreshed course page kept stale learner counts; delete refusals named the wrong learners; "1 characters left"; step names without their language; statistics' slowest lesson, a cut German label and the facilitator view at 320 px; the funnel dropped learners on a course's own lessons. Integration (a review of the whole release with four lenses, 11 findings, plus two found while integrating): the *Add guided practice / applied scenario / quick check* slot added a lesson to a live course unasked, even after the cut-off (major); under the kill switch a course of its own lessons could be finished through standard ones (major); a save could enrol someone in a never-published draft; the outbox could come back over an answer saved elsewhere and outlived its learner; an emptied course read "Completed all"; removing a lesson's only content didn't say the lesson leaves learners' course; the sectioned view counted items as questions, said a quick check's need twice and named two regions alike; a scenario's follow-up dropped focus; "course opened" kept any text; a practice or scenario hint was said twice; a badge written twice at once logged an error.
- `[FIX]` Found by the final verification's load run: the recorded walk's video lesson sometimes lacked "This video pauses to ask you a quick question." A lesson video the page arrives with starts loading before the page's scripts run; when its length was known before the player listened (a busy device, late scripts), the player never learned it, so the questions' place on the timeline and that line never showed. It already happened on Release 0 and 1 (the same code). The player now reads a length that is already there (`followNativeVideo`). A new end-to-end test holds the page's scripts back until the video has its length: red on the build before the fix 4 times out of 4.

The server logged only the deliberate errors — 12 "No signed-in learner", 5 "No signed-in user" (tests of a lost session) and 2 refused sign-ins — the same kinds as Release 1's own suite run the same night (203/203; the loop itself: four full runs 163/163, one with the same dashboard-link failure, one stopped early after a walk's save hadn't reached the database 2 s later at load ~60 · F-E1 and F-E4 red on Release 1 with Release 1's own schema (completion cleared; - `[NOTE]` Known server-log lines of the e2e suite, each harmless:
  - `TypeError: controller[kState].transformAlgorithm is not a function`, logged with a digest, 1–3 times a run. It is Node 22.19's web streams: when the reading side of a stream is cancelled while a transform is running, the writes still queued fail with this TypeError instead of the cancel reason. It is reproduced in plain Node. In the app it comes when the browser leaves a page whose responses are still streaming: timestamped runs of B-E1's steps put each one at the navigation that left the profile step's page, with its server action and prefetches in flight. It never comes for a response that is read to its end.
  - Auth.js `SessionTokenError` (cause: no user in the session callback), once a run, when a test deletes a person whose closing page still asks for its session; that page is answered as signed out.
  - `P2003` on an audit row written after a test's clean-up deleted its user.
  - The deliberate "No signed-in learner/user" and `AccessDenied` lines, as before.
- `[NOTE]` E2E on this machine (system Chrome, no Playwright browser download): Chrome's crash handler starts in a process group of its own and inherits Chrome's stderr. When it outlived Chrome, a Playwright worker never finished stopping ("worker did not exit within 300000ms after stop"), and the run exited 1 although every test passed. The local Playwright configuration now starts one Chrome for the run, and its workers connect to it. CI and `playwright.config.ts` are unchanged, apart from the `last` project's pattern.

  - **full E2E 879 passed, 2 skipped** (F-E1a and F-E1b, by design) in 12.8 min on two workers, exit 0. By project: chromium 868, cutoff 3, switches 4, last 4.
  - An earlier full run on the same build: 874 passed, 1 failed, 4 not run. The failure was a cut-off test's clean-up past its 30 s; the 4 not run were the `last` project, which waits for it. Those 7 then passed, and the second run above was clean.
  - `db-backed-progress.spec.ts` three times on one worker: 140 passed, 1 failed, 30 not run, then **171/171**. The failure was "a guest who builds progress then signs in keeps it server-side": the enrolment had the scenario but no completion within 15 s. It is the same intermittent failure seen on 20 and 21 Oct, and 20/20 alone.
  - `r3-scoping.spec.ts` five times: **45/45**.

### Release 3 — integration: the organisation rule for every new learner, a guardian's permission per organisation, migration D, the runbooks (2026-10-22)
On `r3` after the integration (spec 17.4, 18.3). Nothing pushed (D100).
- `[FIX]` `f66d7a7` The profile step's organisation rule (spec 5.4 (c)) holds for anyone who becomes a learner after it: made LEARNER only (`setMemberRoles`), or given LEARNER again after Remove (`ensureLearnerMembership`, `completeProfile`, `addMember`). `releaseIfCountryElsewhere` releases an organisation a partner admin set when the person's country is mapped to another organisation or to none, in the same transaction as the role change, logged `profile.organisation_released`. An ADMIN's, the country's and the backfill's organisations, and an unfinished profile's, are kept.

- `[DECISION]` A guardian's permission belongs to the organisation that recorded it. A change of organisation clears both records (`setPersonOrganisation` in code; migration D's trigger in the database). Manage people says when an invited under-16's permission isn't recorded by their organisation; that organisation records its own by adding them again with the tick. `addMember` asks for no guardian's permission only for a finished profile that isn't of the invited-with-permission rule.

Each statement is a transaction of its own that locks one table at most (`lock_timeout` 5 s) and can run again. `scripts/r3-check.ts` checks D and both triggers, and its rolled-back smoke test shows each working. After P3009, `migrate-deploy.mjs` says whether the migration Prisma names is one transaction (nothing applied) or commits statement by statement (may be partly applied), and how to resolve it.

### Release 3 — privacy and governance (2026-10-22)
Owner's request: "Start Release 3" — the release that readies the platform for the 1 December pilot with real learners: who sees whose data, the Comp Card as the learner's own, a profile step with the Terms and the age rule, statistics of real learners only, test accounts and the go-live clean-up, data rights complete, German and Greek switched on per course, YouTube only on request, safeguarding contacts, legal texts and security headers. Specified first (spec, decisions D71–D101, owner confirmations), then built as a Foundation (data, the scoping module, the rules, the test fixtures and the table of every server action) and six streams — A people and organisations · B profile step and Comp Card · C staff views and statistics · D data rights and clean-up · E courses, languages and media · F legal, safeguarding and headers — each built repro-first, reviewed through two lenses (correctness and security; accessibility, wording and translation) and corrected; merged in the order C, A, D, F, E, B; integrated (Release 2 merged, I-E1 to I-E3); reviewed across the whole release with four lenses and fixed; accepted with the negative controls and the release check on a fresh database. Nothing pushed (D100). Deploy planned for Fri 20 November 2026.
- `[BUILD]` **One profile per person, one writer of the organisation** (D72, D73, D101). `ProjectProfile` holds a person's country, organisation, profile-step answers and test-account mark; it is the only thing scoping reads. Countries map to organisations as data an ADMIN sets on Organisations & cohorts (default: Germany → Pro Arbeit, Cyprus → Synthesis, Greece → University of Macedonia, Belgium and Other → DIESIS). `setPersonOrganisation` is the only code that writes an organisation: it unshares the Comp Card, clears other organisations' cohorts and a guardian's permission recorded by the old organisation, records who set it, and logs it; database triggers do the same for any other writer.
- `[BUILD]` **Who sees whom** (D74, D75, D77). One server-only module, `src/lib/scope.ts`, decides every staff read and every staff write about another person; the scope is a required argument and "none" matches nothing. ADMINs see the whole project; partner admins and facilitators their own organisation, and only with `R3_PARTNER_ACCESS=on`; staff with no organisation, editors and learners no learner data. Roles and the organisation are read on every request; the session carries only id, email and name (D97).
- `[BUILD]` **The partner admin** (D76): includes Editor and Facilitator; adds, invites, changes and removes its own organisation's learners and facilitators who hold nothing else; gives only those two roles; never sees an ADMIN; claims only an account that holds nothing; changes no country or organisation. Only an ADMIN gives the role.
- `[BUILD]` **The profile step** (D78, D79) at `/welcome`, asked for in the hub and the five workspace layouts: a first name or nickname, the country, the Terms (version and time stored), the age statement (`AGE_RULE`, default 16; or invited with a guardian's permission recorded by the organisation), the privacy notice linked, a consent box built and off (`PROFILE_CONSENT`). The learner role, and any saving of learning, wait for it; guest work moves into an account only when the person chooses "Add it to my account".
- `[BUILD]` **The Comp Card** (D80–D82): "Only me" by default and for every existing card; shared only by the learner's choice with their organisation's facilitators, recorded with its date, organisation and wording version; filtered on the server; 2,000 characters per field, refused not cut; the mentor option gone; unshared by any change of organisation. Scenario choices and results stay visible to the learner's organisation, and learners are told what staff always see.
- `[BUILD]` **Statistics of real learners** (D83, D84): never anyone who holds or held a staff role (`StaffHistory`, filled by a trigger), never a test account, and from `STATS_FROM` (1 Dec 2026). The page says what it counts and why it is empty.
- `[BUILD]` **Data rights and the clean-up** (D85–D89): Admin → Data for an ADMIN: find, export and erase with a request reference, mark and reset test accounts, review lists, the log, and the logged go-live clean-up run (dry run first). Erasure is one transaction and complete; the data download says what it holds and signs notes by organisation; staff notes outlive their author, unsigned. `prisma/purge-demo.mjs` is gone; `docs/data-management.md` is the runbook.
- `[BUILD]` **German and Greek per course** (D90): a "version ready" switch per language and created course, for a partner admin or an ADMIN, audited; until it is on, learners get the course in English with a note and `lang` marking; English complete before a first publish.
- `[BUILD]` **Media** (D91, D92): YouTube only after the viewer presses play, then youtube-nocookie; videos, captions, posters and documents only from the platform's own store (`BLOB_PUBLIC_HOST`); a removed file queued and deleted by the admin run after 7 days when nothing uses it.
- `[BUILD]` **Safeguarding** (D93): contacts per country set by an ADMIN; a box with 112 on learner and account pages and the profile step, `/help`, the footer link, a line beside every reflection.
- `[BUILD]` **Legal texts and headers** (D94, D95): versioned legal files in a Markdown subset, interim texts true to the platform, a placeholder guard; a content security policy, `X-Frame-Options`, `nosniff`, `strict-origin-when-cross-origin` and a Permissions-Policy on every response.
- `[BUILD]` **Sign-in and demo** (D96–D98): rate-limit keys stored as keyed hashes, staff-only limits failing closed; demo sign-in opt-in with a private code and never on production.
- `[MIGRATION]` Four additive migrations: `20261120090000_r3_role_partner_admin`, `20261120090100_r3_people_privacy_content` (one transaction, `lock_timeout` 5 s), `20261120090200_r3_course_project_columns`, `20261120090300_r3_sharing_and_guardian_triggers`. `scripts/r3-check.ts` checks the result (counts only). `migrate-deploy.mjs` says, for a failed migration, whether it applied nothing or may be partly applied.
- `[DECISION]` D71–D101 recorded in DECISIONS.md as built, with the changes made during the build. The owner's twelve confirmations (spec 16.1) apply as their defaults. The consortium's (Q16–Q19, Q24, Q25, Q27, Q28), DIESIS's (O-1, A12) and counsel's answers are still to come; the build follows the defaults the reply names, and each decision says what changes if an answer differs. Owner, 21 Oct: Release 4 started on branches of its own and rebases on `r3`.
- `[SECURITY]` Enforced on the server and tested from outside the screens: the scope guard test reads the sources for any read, relation include, raw query or organisation write that bypasses the scoping module (D75); the action table calls every exported server action as other organisations' staff, partner admins, editors, learners and no one, and compares the database before and after (`e2e/r3-actions.spec.ts`; a new export without a row fails the unit suite); FD-E1 to FD-E11 and the stream specs; and the negative controls below. Weaknesses found by the reviews stay in the private drafts folder until their fix is live (D100).
- `[FIX]` The reviews' findings, each fixed with a test that failed first: Foundation (three review rounds), the six streams (57 findings: C 8, A 10, D 12, F 6, E 7, B 14), the merges (6), the integration's open items (6) and the cross-cutting review (23 findings, four of them major). Among the rules they added: a claim never takes a person the profile step just placed elsewhere; an address on `ADMIN_EMAILS` is an ADMIN's to add; a partner admin deletes a cohort only when it is shown everyone in it; the organisation rule holds for anyone who becomes a learner later; a guardian's permission belongs to the organisation that recorded it; a card's privacy written as anything but shared unshares it, whatever writes it; the content security policy and the media rule admit the same one store; every English-only staff screen is marked as English under `/de` and `/el`; course text carries its language wherever a German or Greek page shows English.
- `[TEST]` **Negative controls (spec 18.3), at integration.** Two throwaway local branches from `r3` (`c89f7a0`, with the guard tests as `815ed2c` commits them), never pushed, never merged, deleted afterwards; each built through the build lock against a fresh database, with the eleven Release 3 specs that hold guards or scoping (actions, scoping, switches, people, data, language, media, safeguarding, comp-card, statistics, profile; 591 tests) run against it:
  - **(a) scope control:** `getViewer` gives the whole project to every staff role and `inScope` answers true: **52 red**, 539 green.
  - **(b) role control:** a partner admin holds ADMIN's powers (`hasRole`, `seesWholeProject`, `grantableRoles`): **54 red**, 537 green.
  - **Every guard is red on its own control: 37 "(guard)" tests on (a), 17 "(role guard)" tests on (b); none stays green.** The integration's first controls (the entry above) left four guard tests green on both (A-E10, A-E11's claim beside the profile step, and the partner admin's 320 px and axe tests); B-E5, not in that run, checked only the organisation stored, which neither control changes. Each now also checks whose people the viewer is shown, and was red on (a) before it was committed. A-E12's country and organisation test is refused by a role, so it is labelled a role guard (red on (b)).
  - Scope guards, red on (a): the action table's 12 partner-admin rows of `admin.ts` and `facilitator.ts` (`addMember`, `assignCohort`, `createCohort`, `deleteCohort`, `removeMember`, `resendInvitation`, `setGuardianSharing`, `setMemberRoles`, `updateCohort`, `updatePersonProfile`, `saveObservation`, `recordValidation`); A-E1 (2), A-E2, A-E3, A-E4, A-E5, A-E9 (2), A-E10, A-E11 (4), A-E12 (4), the partner admin's counts, 320 px and axe tests; B-E5; FD-E8, FD-E9, FD-E10; C-E2. All but A-E10 and B-E5 are red on (b) as well.
  - Role guards, red on (b) and green on (a): `createOrganisation`, `deleteOrganisation`, `setCountryMapping`, the seven `admin-data.ts` rows, `repairOutline`, `saveSafeguardingContacts`, the admin export route, D-E3's and D-E5's partner-admin tests, F-E2's direct save, and A-E12's country and organisation change.
  - Unlabelled tests red too: on (a) every `facilitator.ts` row for FA, FB, FN, FI, FD-E2 to FD-E5, FD-E7, C-E4 and A-E3's cohort rule; on (b) A-E7's partner-admin call and A-E3's cohort rule.
  - Not in the controls, each for a reason: F-E1a's placeholder guard (skipped until counsel's first final text; `src/data/legal.test.ts` refuses placeholders) and Release 2's A-E5 guard (`r2-resume.spec.ts`, not about scope or roles).
- `[VERIFY]` **Release check (spec 18.7), on `815ed2c`** (the application code of `fd3b5f2`; `b01c514` adds documents and a document test), each on a fresh local Postgres made by `migrate-deploy.mjs` (all 18 migrations), the seed and the badge backfill:
  - tsc ✓ eslint ✓ **4,825 unit** ✓ (210 files; 4 skipped: the `r3-check` database tests, **4/4** on an empty throwaway database);

Two earlier full runs of the same build: the first lost its shared browser after four minutes (773 passed, then every test failed with "Browser closed"); the second had 883 passed, 1 failed, 4 not run (the `last` project, which waits): F-E2's error summary not yet focused when checked, which then passed 50 of 50 on one worker;

Within Release 2 + 150 ms;

- `[NOTE]` **Known limits after Release 3** (spec 20, as they stand):
  - Time and video answers recorded before 1 December in a running-total row are left out of statistics whole, including time added later.
  - Former staff who left no trace before 20 November count from 1 December unless marked as test accounts.
  - Course files are public by link; a removed file stays reachable by its link until the next admin run after 7 days; a restored database can point at a file the run deleted.
  - Language coverage ignores extra content, step names, video text and documents; within a language switched on, video questions and documents stay in one language.
  - A course written first in German or Greek can't be first-published until its English is complete.
  - Partner admins edit every course; staff screens other than Support learners stay English (marked as English), and so do the video, document, text and block editors inside Manage courses and the Comp Card wording (marked as English, the text written in them with its own language: the entry below).
  - SENIC's admins see no Comp Card text; a DIESIS admin sees only the cards shared with DIESIS.
  - Learners read staff notes only in their data download (JSON); the account page says so, without a count.
  - Sessions and sign-in links are purged by an admin run, not on a schedule.
  - No rollback below Release 3 after Wed 25 November, and before it only after the pre-rollback step (GO-LIVE §11).
  - A Release 2 tab open across the deploy saves Comp Card text but can't share, its over-length edits aren't saved, and it still moves guest work into an account unasked once the profile is complete (no real learners before 1 December).
  - A partner admin's question before deleting a cohort counts only the members it sees; the refusal comes when it presses Delete.
  - "Add it to my account" (D37, D69, D79) as it stands:
    - one text too long to add holds back every part until it is shortened, a part the account keeps of its own included;
    - work a tab still a guest saves while it is being added stays in the browser, and is said not to have been added; where the account then has progress in that course, the course is kept there;
    - a request whose answer was lost, sent again after another account signed in on the browser: the work is in the first account, the page says nothing was added and offers it to the second, which can add a copy;
    - a sharing choice made in another signed-in tab in the moment before that tab has read the account's card again (well under a second after the work was added) is about the card the account then holds;
    - with the learner player's kill switch on, a created course moves with the completion a save of the same lessons keeps, and its badge stays in the browser as not added;
    - from a guest store the page didn't write (damaged, or edited by hand), answers the server can't read or refuses are left out of the course that moves.
  - Of two pages editing the same Comp Card field, the last save of that field stays (D80).
- `[NOTE]` Known server-log lines of the e2e suite, each harmless and each a deliberate or understood kind: "No signed-in learner" and "No signed-in user" (tests of a lost session and the action table's no-session rows), `AccessDenied` (refused sign-ins), `TypeError: controller[kState].transformAlgorithm is not a function` (Node 22.19's web streams, when a navigation leaves a page still streaming), Auth.js `SessionTokenError` (a test deleting a person whose closing page still asks for its session), `P2003` on a row written after a test's clean-up deleted its user, and the `BLOB_PUBLIC_HOST` notice of a build without it.
- `[NOTE]` Open, outside the build: the merge of `main` on Wed 11 Nov and the last merge point on Thu 19 Nov; the owner's Neon rehearsal (GO-LIVE §1b, with I-E3 on the branch) on Tue 17 Nov; counsel's English texts (F-E1b, `@launch`, a go/no-go item) and the German and Greek ones; the partners' review of the German and Greek drafts (Wed 18 Nov); the safeguarding contacts; the consortium's answers (Tue 27 Oct) and the lawful basis (Fri 30 Oct); two wording questions (the English `reflection.placeholder2`, and spec 9.3's note that the completion screen and the Comp Card now mark their language). The intermittent learner-data test ("a guest who builds progress then signs in keeps it server-side": the completion not yet in the database within 15 s, seen on 20, 21 and 22 Oct) is left for its owner.
