### Release 3 — polish, after its review: guest work added where the account has its own, the browser's copy removed only as it was read, a Scope that can't be copied or changed (2026-10-22)
On `r3` after the polish above. Nothing pushed (D100).
- `[FIX]` `c9c02bc` (D79 as built; D69 amended, a default for the owner to confirm) **"Add it to my account" fills what the account lacks, and "Added" means it is there.**
  - **The course.** The guest's course is sent in fill mode also into a course the account already has progress in. The account's lessons and answers stay; the guest's lessons, answers and completion are added (D37's "the account wins", answer by answer).
  - **The Comp Card.** It is filled field by field (`cardFill`), and the confidence only into a card with nothing in it.
  - **A shared Comp Card** gets nothing: moving never shares (D80). The guest's card stays in the browser, and the page says to set the card to "Only me" to add it, or to remove it.
  - **What the page says.** Where the account had answers of its own, the page says it kept them.
  - **The browser's copy** is removed only if it still holds what the move read (compare and remove). What a tab still a guest saves meanwhile stays, and is offered again. A second "Add" fills the rest.
  - **Records.** New wording in en, de and el (`guestWork.addedOwn`, `addedMore`, `cardShared`). DECISIONS (D69, D79) and DATA-PROTECTION row 12 state the rule.
- `[SECURITY]` `04140a5` (SS4, as built now) **A `Scope` can't be copied with its fields changed or changed in place, and the guard reads types, not names.**
  - **The brand** is a class with a private field that only `src/lib/scope.ts` names. A spread copies no private field, so neither a literal nor a changed copy of a real scope type-checks outside the module.
  - **Read-only and frozen.** A Scope's fields are read-only. Every scope the module makes is frozen, as are the viewer `getViewer` answers, its roles and the shared "nobody".
  - **The scope guard** reads `src/` with the type checker, in one program with this project's tsc options. It fails, outside tests and the module, for:
    - the test-only factory;
    - an assertion to a Scope, or to a type holding one, of a value that isn't one, whatever the type is spelled as (an alias, `Viewer["scope"]`, `ReturnType<…>`, `Parameters<…>[0]`, an import type);
    - a value of type any or never where a Scope is expected;
    - an object literal there;
    - `Object.assign`, `defineProperty` and the like on a Scope;
    - a call of a generic function that asserts to its own type parameter, when a Scope comes out of it.
- `[TEST]` Each change's tests ran red first, on the code or the build before it:

  - scopes: `src/lib/scope-brand.test.ts`'s new case (the spread copy, the change in place and the copy as an argument compiled), the guard's new meta-tests (no hit for the five spellings, any, never, a literal under `@ts-expect-error`, `Object.assign`), and `src/lib/scope.test.ts`'s frozen scopes and viewers (4 red on the module before).

### Release 3 — polish, second review: guest work added as the account is when it lands, a guest's page kept the guest's, the scope guard's wider rules (2026-10-22)
On `r3` after the polish above. Nothing pushed (D100).
- `[FIX]` `225d2de` (D79 as built) **"Add it to my account" judges what the account lacks as the account is when each write lands, and the page then shows what the server holds.**
  - **The Comp Card** is filled on the server under its row's lock (`saveCompCard` in fill mode, `compCardFill` in `src/lib/comp-card-visibility.ts`): a text only where the card has none, the confidence only into a card with nothing in it, nothing into a card shared with the learner's facilitators (D80). The page takes the card as the server answers it, keeps a field changed on the page meanwhile, and sends nothing else again.
  - **The course being played** is read again once the guest's course moved into it (`courseReread`): the server's progress, with only what the page did that the server didn't have on top. A course opened later loads the server's progress as it is (`courseAfterLoad`, the guest's copy on the page left out).
  - **The standard quick check** is the account's once it has one: answers, a check, "Try again" (saved empty) or its lesson done (D49: the latest check counts), as a quick check of the course's own already was (`merge-server.ts`).
  - **Another account's session.** A write refused as another account's stops the move; the browser keeps all of it, and the page reads its session again and offers the work to whoever is signed in. The browser's copy is touched only while the account that started the move is signed in.
  - **The browser keeps only what didn't move** (`guestStoreLeft`): a Comp Card held back from a shared card, a write that never got through, as it was read (compare and swap). A later "Add" moves only that.
  - **What the page says.** "Where your account already had an answer of its own, it keeps it" only where the account kept an answer or a confidence of its own over a different one of the guest's (the server reports where in the course: `kept`). With no work left in the browser (added or removed in another tab), "Nothing was added: there is no work on this browser to add any more." (`guestWork.nothing`, en/de/el).
  - A guest course too big for one save moves in several, each in fill mode.
- `[FIX]` `e5eaddd` (D79) **A guest's page stays the guest's when someone signs in in another tab** of the browser (where the sign-in link opened): what is on it stays (the answers pressed, the score, the place in the course), Continue goes on, what is done there is written to this browser's guest store only, and the account doesn't load into it. A status says so without moving the focus and offers "Reload this page"; reloaded, the account's page asks whether to add the work (`guestWork.signedInElsewhere`, `reload`, en/de/el). A guest's page with no work on it takes the account up at once and starts over for it. `sessionChange` answers `"signedInElsewhere"` for it.
- `[SECURITY]` `2749095` (SS4) **A `Scope` is made only by `src/lib/scope.ts`, and the guard says so for more ways round it.** The scope of chosen roles and settings (`scopeFor`) isn't exported; tests reach it as `testOnlyScopeFor`, refused outside tests like `testOnlyScope`. The guard also fails, outside tests and the module, for a Scope copied by `Object.assign` and the like (however the function is named), a call of a generic function of `src/` that makes its type parameter's value (a spread, `Object.assign`, an any, an assertion) when a Scope comes out of it, an any or a never inside an array, a tuple or a promise where a Scope is expected, a type predicate or an overload answering a Scope, and a server action that takes a Scope or a Viewer from its caller.

### Release 3 — polish, third: a Scope checked when it is used, a Comp Card text too long to add kept and shortened (2026-10-22)
On `r3` after the polish above. Nothing pushed (D100).
- `[SECURITY]` `07e614a` (SS4, D75 as built) **Every function of `src/lib/scope.ts` that takes a Scope checks, when it is called, that the module made it.** The module keeps each scope it makes, by identity (a `WeakSet`, so a request's scopes go with it); any other value throws `NotAScopeError` before anything of it is read: no filter is built, wider or narrower, and no row is counted (fail closed). The message names nothing of the value. So an object from JSON or posted by the browser to a server action, a spread copy, `Object.assign` onto a new object, a structured clone, a proxy or an object made from a real scope's prototype, and a value a generic class or callback built are never read as a scope, whatever the type check and the guard saw; `requireAdminProject` checks the viewer's scope the same way. The scope guard and the type brand stay as they were, and gain:
  - a server action that takes a Viewer through a library type (`Pick<Viewer, "scope">`, `Partial<Viewer>`, `Omit`, `Readonly`) is refused like one that takes a Viewer;
  - every "use server" module of `src/`, not only those in `src/app/actions`, exports its actions where they are declared: no export list (also under another name), `export *`, `export default` of a value or `export =` (`use-server-exports.test.ts`), so the guard finds every action;
  - a generic class whose method makes its class's type parameter's value, and a generic function that hands what it makes to a callback, are left to the run-time check, which refuses what they build (`scope-runtime.test.ts`).
  - Cost: a `WeakSet` lookup, 2 to 6 ns a call. Over 2,000,000 calls of each builder, seven rounds against the module before it: `learnerUsersInScope` 46.2 → 49.6 ns, `peopleInScope` 22.9 → 25.1 ns, `statisticsMembershipsInScope` 67.0 → 72.7 ns, `cohortsInScope` 11.8 → 16.3 ns; `inScope` (its count stood in for) 366 → 354 ns, within the noise. A page calls a handful of builders, each before a query of milliseconds.
- `[FIX]` `edf4d64` (D79, D80 as built) **A Comp Card text of the guest's longer than a card holds is never dropped by "Add it to my account".** Where the account's card has no text in that field, the rest of the card moves (and the confidence, into a card with nothing in it), and that text alone stays in the browser (`cardTooLong`, `guestStoreLeft`); the page doesn't say "Added". It says which answer, by the Comp Card's own words, and what to do: "Everything else was added to your account. Your Comp Card answer to “What was difficult” wasn't added: it is longer than 2,000 characters. It stays on this browser: shorten it above, then add it, or remove it from this browser." (`guestWork.cardTooLong`, en/de/el). The offer shows each such text in a labelled box with its length ("2,105 characters: 105 characters too many.", then "… of 2,000 characters: short enough to add.", said once through a polite live region); what is typed is saved to this browser's guest store at once, that field only (`shortenGuestCard`), so it outlives a reload, and the next "Add" moves it. Where the account's card has a text of its own, the account's stays, as for any text. A card the account shares, a save that didn't get through and another account's session keep the whole card, as before.
- `[TEST]` `0aa7b24` **The e2e "a Comp Card text another device writes while the guest's card is being added" holds the card's write by the guest's text it carries**, not by the fill flag, so it waits for the card's write on any build and fails only on what the account holds after.
- `[TEST]` `7961a80` **E-E1 reads the course's card on Manage courses as shown** (`.filter({ visible: true })`, the suite's rule since 5a6b7a1): while the page streams in, React may hold the server's copy of it hidden for a moment.
- `[DOCS]` D75 and D79 have as-built lines for these rules.
- `[TEST]` Each change's tests ran red first, on the code or the build before it:
  - the scope check: `scope-runtime.test.ts` **18 of 19 red** on the module before (no case refused; the one green is the control that real scopes are accepted); the verifier's V1 guard case (a Viewer through `Pick`, `Partial`, `Omit`, `Readonly`) red with the guard before (no hit); the verifier's V2 (an action exported by a list from `src/lib`) passed by the old `use-server-exports.test.ts`, which read only `src/app/actions`, and refused by the new one; `facilitator.test.ts` now gives the real `inScope` scopes the module made;
  - the Comp Card text: the reviewer's unit test (polish2-claim) red on 15baa89 and green now (with the reviewer's other five); `guest-migration.test.ts` **13 red** on the code before (the long text dropped and the store removed: `left: null`); the new `r3-guest-claim` test red on the 15baa89 build and on 5539eed's, where the browser's copy is gone after "Add" (`null`);
  - the hold by the card's text: on 5539eed's build the test is red **2/2** for the right reason (`wentWell` is the guest's text, not the other device's), and green on 15baa89's;
  - E-E1's card read as shown: red 3/6 on one worker against the 15baa89 build (two cards matched), 8/8 after.

  - tsc ✓ eslint ✓ **4,925 unit** ✓ (215 files, one skipped; 4 tests skipped: the `r3-check` database tests); each commit on its own: tsc, eslint and unit ✓ (`07e614a` 4,914, `edf4d64` 4,925);

### Release 3 — guest work added part by part in one server transaction, a guest's page kept the guest's loads every other page in full (2026-10-22)
On `r3` after the polish above. Nothing pushed (D100).
- `[BUILD]` `c9a1713` (D37, D69, D79, D80) **One server action adds a browser's guest work to the account, part by part, in one transaction** (`claimGuestWork` in `src/app/actions/learner.ts`; what each answer means is in `src/lib/guest-claim.ts`).
  - **The course** moves only into an account with no progress in it, through the save's own transaction (`progressInTx`, taken out of `saveProgress`: the completion, the kill switch's rule for it and its badge). An account with progress in it gets nothing: "already" when it holds all of the guest's, "kept" otherwise. A course nobody can open any more is "unavailable".
  - **The Comp Card** moves only into no card, or a blank one (no answer, the confidence untouched) that isn't shared, and is written as "Only me". The same card is "already" there; a card with something of the account's own is "kept"; a blank shared one gets nothing ("shared"): adding never shares.
  - **Badges** are a union, each given as the award action gives it (`badgeMayBeGiven`, now the award's own check too).
  - **Locks**, in this order: the User row (KEY SHARE); the Course row (KEY SHARE, or UPDATE when the completion may make its badge); the enrolment, then the Comp Card, each inserted unless there, then locked, read and decided as read; the badges, inserted unless there.
  - **Nothing is written** for another account's session, an incomplete profile, a claim that isn't one, a text too long to add (a Comp Card answer over 2,000 characters, a reflection answer over 5,000: named in the answer) or a claim bigger than one save. Each claim's outcome is logged as counts per part, without the learner, the course or a text.
- `[BUILD]` `9f84197` **The claim built from the guest store, and the store once it is answered** (`guestClaimOf`, `storeAfterClaim`, `withText` in `src/store/guest-migration.ts`). A part the server says is in the account leaves the browser only as the claim read it; what was saved to it since stays, and is offered again.
- `[BUILD]` `19afe6a` **"Add it to my account" sends the guest work once, and the page says what became of each part.** The page flushes its waiting saves and sends the store's work in one call, tried again only when it throws (a part already in the account is answered "already", never added twice). The browser's copy then goes per part, whoever is signed in by then, and the page shows what the server holds. It says "Added to your account." or "It's already in your account." when all of it is in the account; otherwise one sentence per part and "What wasn't added stays on this browser until you remove it." (`guestWork.*`, en/de/el).
  - Removed: the move made of several saves (`migrateGuest` and its types, `guestStoreLeft`, `cardFill`, `cardTooLong`, `cardKeptOwn`), `saveCompCard`'s fill mode and `compCardFill`, and the outcomes `addedOwn`, `addedMore`, `cardShared` and `cardTooLong` with their messages. The course's fill mode stays for Release 2 pages open across the deploy (D68).
- `[BUILD]` `a1b061c` **The offer says to add the work before anything else is started in the account** (`guestWork.addFirst`), **and shows every text too long to add to be shortened**: a Comp Card answer or a reflection answer, each in a labelled box with its length against its own limit; what is typed goes to the guest store at once, that text only.
- `[BUILD]` `d07914a` (IL-2b) **A guest's page kept the guest's loads every other page in full, isn't refreshed, and writes the guest store only over what it last wrote or read.** Otherwise it stops writing, says so (`guestWork.changedElsewhere`) and offers a reload. A `storage` listener: a page kept the guest's stops at any change of the guest store in another tab, a guest's page at its removal, and a signed-in page offers the work as the browser holds it now.
- `[TEST]` `789bf02` `e2e/r3-guest-claim.spec.ts` rewritten for the claim (14 tests); `e2e/r3-guest-tab.spec.ts` gains 4 (a finished course and a shared Comp Card opened from a page kept the guest's; the page stops once the work is added in another tab; saves made during the claim stay and are offered again); `db-backed-progress` restated for "already". `17c0ad2`: "Add pressed in two tabs at once" holds both tabs' requests until both are on their way, so the server decides the two claims side by side at any load.
- `[DOCS]` `eac6efe` D69, D79 and D68 as built; DATA-PROTECTION row 12; GO-LIVE §1b's rehearsal adds guest work by hand (step 3).

### Release 3 — the verification's follow-ups: a Comp Card save whose answer is lost is followed by another, a "[" in a quick link's host is read as the URL parser reads it, the clean-up's focus on any device (2026-10-23)
On `r3` after the claim's verification above. Nothing pushed (D100).
- `[FIX]` `736f994` (D80 as built) **A Comp Card save that fails once the page has changed since it was sent is followed by another**, if the page still differs from what the server is then known to hold (`saveCard` in `src/store/card-save.ts`). Its own retry is skipped for the newer save (`pending-save.ts`), which may have been worked out while the server was still known to hold what it sent, and found nothing to send (an answer changed back). The save that follows sends the field whose save wasn't confirmed. Failing, it is tried again as any save is (1 s, 2 s, 4 s, 8 s), then left until the next change, never sent every 700 ms. A page still as the failed save sent it tries that save again, and nothing follows once the learner's data was let go of.
- `[FIX]` `cb20cac` (D55) **Quick links: a "[" anywhere in a web address's host opens "[…]"**, and a "]" closes it, as for the URL parser (`webHost` in `src/lib/rich-text/linkify.ts`). The port starts at the first colon outside the brackets, so a host the parser reads whole is measured whole against DNS's limits (`hostFits`) before it is parsed. `scripts/linkify-cost.ts` measures such hosts against the budget too.
- `[FIX]` `d197eee` (WCAG 2.4.3, as F-E2) **/admin/data: the clean-up's dry run and run move the focus to what they show once it is on the screen** (`Cleanup` in `DataTools.tsx`). The focus moves from an effect after the render that shows it, never from a frame scheduled before that render.
- `[DOCS]` D80's 23 October line: a save follows on its own once a save fails, when the page has changed since that save was sent.

  - `r3-data-last` D-E6, the dry run and the run with Chrome's CPU throttling at 20×, on the d6a39cf build: red 4 of 4 at the dry run's focus. With both checks soft, red 3 of 3: the dry run's focus 2 of 3, the run's 2 of 3.

### Release 2 — a course answer changed back while its save is on its way is saved (2026-10-23)
On `r2` after `983be2d`. Nothing pushed (D100).
- `[FIX]` `da3472b` (D62 as built) **What a course save sends isn't known on the server until that save is confirmed** (`src/store/course-save.ts`; `patchSince`'s `unsure` in `src/lib/learner-progress/patch.ts`): it may still be written while it is on its way, and one that fails (a dropped connection) may have been. An answer a save on its way carries that the page has changed since, and an answer whose save failed until a save of it is confirmed or the course loads again (`courseLoaded`), is sent as the page has it. An answer changed back to what the server was known to hold gets a save of its own: the reflection, the practice, the quick check and the self-assessment alike. A save that fails once the page has changed since it was worked out is followed by another if something is left; failing with the page unchanged, it is tried again (1 s, 2 s, 4 s, 8 s) and then left until the next change, never sent every 700 ms. Once a save is confirmed, another follows only for what no save on its way carries. A save that finds a newer build wrote nothing.
- `[FIX]` `da3472b` (D68 as built) **The outbox keeps, beside an answer whose save failed, what the server holds if it was written** (`alt` in `src/store/outbox.ts`; `restorablePatch`'s `ifWritten`), and brings the answer back over either, never over an answer saved elsewhere.
- `[FIX]` `da3472b` (D62) **A refused answer that comes back unchanged isn't sent again, and the learner is told again**, on every save that leaves it out.
- `[DOCS]` D62's 23 October line.
- `[TEST]` Each change's tests ran red first, on 983be2d:
  - `course-save.test.ts` (the committed file against 983be2d's code, the course load as `demo-state.tsx` did it there): 19 of the 26 new tests red — the account ending with the change while the page shows the answer before it (reflection, practice, self-assessment, quick check; a newer save after the change back; the connection gone for every try), the reloaded or reopened page showing the change, the outbox not bringing the answer back, the learner not told again. Green there, their 7 guards: a slow answer (twice), a lesson finished, a newer build, data let go of, the course loaded again, saves slower than the pause. `patch.test.ts` 2 of 2 and `outbox.test.ts` 1 of 1 new tests red;
  - `e2e/r2-course-save.spec.ts` on the 983be2d build: CS-E1, CS-E3 and CS-E4 red (the account held "changed …" while the page showed "before …"; the practice `shrink` while the page showed `iStatement`; the reloaded page showed "changed …"); CS-E2, a slow answer, green.
- `[VERIFY]` On the local Postgres `seq_r2i_v3`:

  - production build ✓ (through the build lock), no warnings;
  - **full E2E 272/272** in 5.6 min on two workers, exit 0 (chromium 269, cutoff 3);

### Release 3 — Release 2's course save rule merged; a "[" in a quick link's host but at its start isn't an address (2026-10-23)
On `r3` after the verification's follow-ups above. Nothing pushed (D100).
- `[MERGE]` `152e7bb` `r2` at `491e5a5` (Release 2's entry above, `da3472b`: what a course save sends isn't known on the server until that save is confirmed; the outbox keeps what the server holds if a failed save was written; a refused answer coming back unchanged is told again). Conflicts in `src/store/demo-state.tsx`, where the course's load calls `courseLoaded` and keeps Release 3's read again after guest work moved into the course (`courseReread`, its base taken before the load) and Release 3's imports; and in `BUILDLOG.md`, Release 2's entry after Release 3's of the same day. `card-save.ts` and the claim are unchanged.
- `[FIX]` `ac2656a` (D55) **Quick links: a "[" anywhere in a web address's host but at its first character means the text isn't a web address** (`webHost` in `src/lib/rich-text/linkify.ts`), its port included. The URL parser refuses every such host, and reads on from a "[" to the next "]" (or the end) past a "/", "?" or "\" that would otherwise end the host, so such a host is never parsed. A host that starts with "[" is measured as the URL parser reads it, up to the next "]" (or the end, or a fragment) whatever "/", "?" or "\" it holds, with its port after that "]" (`hostFits`). One rule for every place a "[" can stand in a host. `scripts/linkify-cost.ts` measures hosts behind "x[?", "x[/", "x[\", "x[?:]", "x[\:]", "x[?]", "a[?@", "u]@x[?", "a@x[/", "[/", "[?" and "[\" against the budget too.
- `[DOCS]` D71: `r2` merged into `r3` again at `491e5a5`.
- `[TEST]` Each change's tests ran red first:
  - the merge: Release 2's entry above, red on 983be2d; `course-save.ts`, `patch.ts`, `outbox.ts` and `pending-save.ts` are the same at 5b4dfdd as at 983be2d, and on r3 all of Release 2's new tests pass (`r2-course-save` 4/4 on the r3 build);
  - quick links, on 152e7bb: the new `linkify.test.ts` ratio test red (text 0: 135.39 ms against 0.41 ms, 328 times); `linkify-cost.ts` 9 blocks over the budget, the nine forms behind "x[" or "a[" (135.6–141.0 ms per 10,000 characters), the three behind "[" within it.
- `[VERIFY]` What is linked, 152e7bb and after, all alike:
  - c611bd1's 31 linkify tests pass on both, and their 109 `findLinks` and 6 `linkifyInline` inputs give the same links;
  - 153,684 texts of ordinary writing (every line and every file of the repository at c611bd1, the en/de/el messages, Release 4's link corpora) show no difference;
  - so do the 19 texts of real addresses near the limits and of brackets in a path, query, fragment, user name, IPv6 address or host;
  - 43 bracket, at-sign, backslash and percent forms: all within the budget (2 over on 152e7bb).

### Release 2 — a page hidden or left keeps what a course save on its way hasn't confirmed (2026-10-23)
On `r2` after `491e5a5`. Nothing pushed (D100).
- `[FIX]` `ec2f0ae` (D68 as built) **A page hidden or left with a course save on its way, or one that failed, keeps what the server hasn't confirmed in the tab's outbox** (`pageHidden` in `src/store/course-save.ts`, on `pagehide` and on `visibilitychange` to hidden in `src/store/demo-state.tsx`, once the waiting saves are sent). Next sends a page's server actions one at a time: a save handed over while another is on its way waits behind it. Every answer a save on its way carries counts as not confirmed, and beside what the server held the entry keeps what those saves leave as they are written in turn. The page that loads the course next — after a reload, or after a phone discards the hidden tab — brings the answer back over any of them. Synchronous, `sessionStorage` only.
- `[FIX]` `ec2f0ae` (D68 as built) **The outbox keeps beside an answer every value a save not confirmed may have left**, each once, at most eight (`alt` as a list, `OUTBOX_ALT_MAX`; `restorablePatch`'s `ifWritten`); the store keeps what each failed save would leave apart (the first seven and the newest), so after two lost saves of one answer, then a newer build, the answer comes back whichever was written. An entry kept with one value reads as before.
- `[FIX]` `ec2f0ae` **While the outbox holds an entry for a course, it follows what is confirmed**: written again after each confirmed save, each save that finds nothing to send and each change that leaves nothing new; cleared once nothing the server would take is left and no save is on its way.
- `[DOCS]` D68's 23 October line.
- `[TEST]` Red first, on 491e5a5:

Green there, their 5 guards: the change's save not written; its answer lost before the reload; no save on its way; nothing kept for a course with none; an entry with one value. Without the rule that an entry follows what is confirmed, its test is red: the course opened again brings the answer before back over the one given again and confirmed;
  - `e2e/r2-course-save.spec.ts` on the 491e5a5 build: CS-E5 and CS-E6 red (the page loaded again showed "changed …"), with only the held save leaving the page before the reload; CS-E7, a guard, green.

### Release 2 — known limits of course and Comp Card saves: a page that goes with a save on its way (2026-10-24)
On `r2` after `919063a`. Nothing pushed (D100). Documentation only.
- `[DECISION]` The integrator's decision (a), 24 October: Release 2 keeps D37's 13 October acceptance for course and Comp Card saves alike — server actions run one at a time, and nothing sends a save outside that queue — as a known limit. Release 2 is live from 10 to 20 November for staff and test accounts only, and Release 3 closes the gap for both before the pilot (D37 as built, 24 October).
- `[NOTE]` Known limit (course saves): a course save handed over while another is on its way waits behind it in Next's queue, and doesn't leave a page that goes before that one is answered; the tab's outbox brings it back when that tab is reloaded or opens the course again (D68), and nothing does once the tab is closed.
- `[NOTE]` Known limit (Comp Card saves): a Comp Card save handed over while another is on its way waits behind it in Next's queue, and doesn't leave a page that goes before that one is answered, closed or reloaded; nothing keeps it, and the account holds what the save on its way carried.

### Release 4 — links and references (started 2026-10-22)
Release 4 of the plan answering the consortium's change requests, built from its spec of 21 October (deploy planned for Thu 26 November 2026): named links in lesson text, and links kept when pasting from Word and Google Docs (Part A); further-reading links next to a lesson's documents, a language for each document or link, and a references & bibliography list per course (Part B). Built on three local branches taken from `r3`, each one a part as it would ship: `r4` (the shared commit, then Part A), `r4b` (from the shared commit: Part B) and `r4r` (from `r4b`: the references screens), merged into one another only at the checkpoints; none is pushed before the deploy merge (D100, D102). First step: the Foundation-light, the contracts both parts code against.
- `[DECISION]` D102: the early start was confirmed by the owner on 21 October 2026; Release 3's integrator acknowledges it (no change asked of Release 3).
- `[BUILD]` **Shared commit** (`r4`): one address check for every link on the platform (`src/lib/rich-text/link-url.ts`, D103: https, http and mailto with at most a subject; normalised; at most 2,048 characters; no user name or password; a host with a dot; no Outlook safe link; our own file store flagged, by its exact host when it is known); link text against where the link goes, and the Link form's warnings and address completion (`link-text.ts`); our file store's host (`own-file-host.ts`); the link mark (its address only) and version 2 for a text that holds one, in the stored format (D104); `richTextForSave`, a paragraph's save as its own export, unchanged; the references variant of the text editor (D109); and the table of hostile links every saving action and page is tried with (spec 10.3), checked against the address check. Nothing users see changes.
- `[BUILD]` **Part B commit** (`r4b`): the `LessonLink` and `CourseBibliography` tables and `LessonDocument.languages` (D107–D109), migration `20261126090000_r4_further_reading`; `scripts/r4-check.ts` (counts only); the switches `R4_FURTHER_READING` and `R4_ITEM_LANGUAGE` (D111); which documents and links a learner gets by language, and which language's references list (`item-language.ts`, `bibliography.ts`), with Part B's types; the message keys of spec 6.1–6.2 in English, German and Greek (German and Greek drafts for partner review); end-to-end fixtures for a Release 4 course and another project, seeded on either build's database. `r4r` is branched from it.
- `[DECISION]` The migration is one explicit transaction (`BEGIN` … `COMMIT`, with `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '5s'` first), as Release 3's 20261120090100: Prisma 7.8 applies a migration's statements one by one and commits each on its own otherwise (on a throwaway database, a migration whose second statement failed left its first statement's table behind). One transaction keeps what the spec relies on: every lock is held to the end, so the new tables come first and the change to `LessonDocument` last, and a failure leaves nothing applied for `prisma migrate resolve --rolled-back` (spec 9.6, step 2a).
- `[DECISION]` Release 3's migration test checks that its migrations sort after every earlier one; a later release's migrations (named `_r4_` onward) sort after them by design.
- `[VERIFY]` **R4-F1** on the build Release 4 rolls back to (`r3` at 4ddf60a, started from its own worktree on its own database; `e2e/r4-f1.spec.ts` with `E2E_R4_ROLLBACK_BUILD=1`): a direct `saveLessonNarrative` call with a version-2 paragraph holding a link ("the guide" → `https://example.org/guide`, text "Read the guide (https://example.org/guide) today.") answered `ok: true` and stored the block as `{ kind: "paragraph", text: "Read the guide (https://example.org/guide) today." }`, with nothing of the link mark; a version-2 paragraph written straight into the database was drawn from its `text`, "https://example.org/guide" a quick link opening in a new tab ("(opens in a new tab)"), and no link named "the guide": **2/2**. So the code Release 4 rolls back to keeps a linked paragraph's address in its text, on screen and on a save (spec 3.3, 7.2).
- `[VERIFY]` Foundation-light's acceptance (spec 9.1). `r4`: tsc ✓ eslint ✓ **4,904 unit** ✓ (3 skipped: database-gated), messages parity included · U1 and U2 green, and the whole existing suite green unchanged · production build ✓. `r4b`: tsc ✓ eslint ✓ **4,952 unit** ✓ (7 skipped: database-gated), U14–U17 and messages parity included · production build ✓ · on a fresh database the migration applies after Release 3's three (18 ms) · the drift check (`prisma migrate diff` from that database to `schema.prisma`) is empty · `scripts/r4-check.ts`: all checks hold on the migrated database; on a Release 3 database it names the two tables and the column and exits 1; its database test 4/4 · **full E2E 875/875** on `r4b`'s production build and database, two workers (872 in the run, 2 skipped by design: F-E1a until counsel's final texts, F-E1b `@launch`; the 3 of the `last` project, held back by Playwright's "worker did not exit" teardown errors, which belong to no test, run on their own: 3/3) · the fixtures of a Release 4 course and of another project seed and clean up on both builds' databases · negative control of the address check (it accepting any string, never committed): every string row of the hostile-link table fails (19 of its 24 tests; the other 5 test non-strings, labels and the over-limit block), and so do 68 of U1's 98 and 2 of U2's 21.
- `[DECISION]` A D102 departure (its condition 3), accepted under its condition 4: Part A changed one line of `src/components/course/ReflectionIntro.test.ts`, a test of a component on Stream E's language-marking list (spec 9.7), so that its row for a format version the introduction doesn't read uses version 3 (version 2 is now one it reads). No Release 3 branch has changed the file; Release 4 resolves any conflict in it at its rebases.
- `[BUILD]` A Foundation-light commit on the shared contracts (`4615b51`, made on `r4`, to be cherry-picked onto `r4b` and `r4r`): link text is read as learners see it (characters that show nothing don't split an address; compatibility forms read as what they show; an address that couldn't be a link still counts), and a web address's host is accepted only as every browser and the server read it alike (`link-host.ts`); an email link's subject holds no "&", "?" or "#".
- `[DECISION]` D104 also covers a bare host shown in link text (the owner's decision of 22 October 2026, the safer reading): text that shows "www.bank.example", "bank.example/login" or "paypal.com" shows an address of that site, so a link whose text shows another site is refused on save, in the Link form, on paste and as a further-reading title, and named in the editor at once, exactly as for a full address. A name is read as a host when it starts with "www." or a path or a port follows it, or when it ends in a top-level domain (the Public Suffix List's ICANN section, and the names reserved for examples, tests and local networks); an ordinary word with a dot is not one: a version ("v1.2"), an abbreviation ("e.g.", "z.B.", "κ.λπ."), the end of a sentence, a file's name ("report.pdf").
- `[BUILD]` `4615b51` cherry-picked onto `r4b` and `r4r`. A third Foundation-light commit on the shared contracts (`dd37196`, made on `r4`, cherry-picked onto `r4b` and `r4r` the same day): the bare host of D104 above (`link-text.ts`, new `top-level-domains.ts`), with two link labels and three further-reading titles showing another site as a bare host added to the hostile table; on `r4b`, a further-reading title is compared with its link by `linkTextProblem` alone.
- `[BUILD]` The text editor tells each text what it can do about text that can't be saved (`RT_ADVICE`; `d295b8a`): a paragraph or key message is split into two blocks, a reflection lesson's introduction is shortened, the references list is shortened or written again on a reloaded page; `richTextForSave` takes the same advice, which the references action asks for. The references editor offers no indent (`6fed794`): every entry hangs alike by the list's layout, so it has no Indent buttons, and no indent step comes from a shortcut, a paste or a stored entry; a list point still moves in and out. Both made on `r4` and cherry-picked onto `r4b` and `r4r` the same day.
- `[DECISION]` D104 compares sites, not exact hosts (the owner's decision of 22 October 2026): a site is a registrable domain by the ICANN section of the Public Suffix List, the public suffix a host ends in ("org", "co.uk", "gov.gr") and one label more. So "europa.eu" over a link to ec.europa.eu and "Wikipedia.org" over en.wikipedia.org show the link's site, and "x.com" over another site doesn't; a name of another site that a host shows in front of the link's ("paypal.com" in "paypal.com.evil.example", over a link to evil.example) is that site, shown, while the link's own host, shown as it is, shows where it goes. Full addresses, bare hosts and email domains are compared alike. The public suffixes of more than one label are a table in `top-level-domains.ts`, generated on 22 October 2026 by `scripts/public-suffixes.ts` from tldts-icann 7.4.2 (published 30 September 2026; a development dependency through lighthouse, nothing of which reaches the app): every rule of each of 204 top-level domains (co.uk, com.gr, ac.cy and ac.at among them, 11 KB); the five whose rules name towns, regions and states by the hundred (it, jp, no, pl, us) are left out, and a host under them is compared by its exact host. A unit test checks the table against tldts-icann's rules and its `getDomain` while 7.4.2 is installed. The browser chunk that carries the link rules grew from 33.2 KB to 46.7 KB (20.6 KB gzipped).
- `[DECISION]` A file's name that ends in a top-level domain ("course-materials.zip", "intro.mov", "README.md") is read as a host and refused when the link opens another site, which fails safe (the owner's decision of 22 October 2026). The text editor's problem line and a save's refusal ("the link text “course-materials.zip” looks like a web address, but the link opens another one. Use a few words instead, or show the real address."), the Link form and the further-reading form say so whenever every address a text shows is a name read only by the top-level domain it ends in; text showing an address with a scheme, "www.", a path or an email address keeps "shows a different address than the link opens".
- `[BUILD]` Reading a bare host (D104): an abbreviation is no host, a title or a company form before the last label ("Dr.med.vet.", "Univ.Prof.", "Hon.Prof.", "Müller GmbH & Co.KG") or one letter followed by a label in title case ("M.Sc.", "B.Com.", "B.Tech.", "M.St."), while "x.com", "X.com", "t.co", "Paypal.com" and "PAYPAL.COM" stay hosts; a last label of Cyrillic or Greek letters that look like Latin ones is read as the top-level domain it shows ("paypal.соm", "PAYPAL.СОМ", "paypal.cοm"), and a top-level domain of another script stays itself ("ευ"); a host wrapped for emphasis at a word's start is read ("_paypal.com_", "~paypal.com~"), and a name inside a path or a word still belongs to it ("docs/report.de", "my_site.de").
- `[BUILD]` A fourth Foundation-light commit on the shared contracts (`cdacf4a`: `link-text.ts`, `top-level-domains.ts`, `scripts/public-suffixes.ts`, U2, the hostile table with three more misleading labels, six more misleading titles and `ACCEPTED_LABELS`), and `1b3dffb` (`narrative.ts`: `sanitizeNarrativeBlocks` names a reflection lesson's introduction as its editor does when asked, `"reflection-intro"`), made on `r4` and cherry-picked onto `r4b` (`b9011ca`, `90e87ff`) and `r4r` (`f3ee377`, `129fe7d`) the same day. On `r4`: the editor's wording (`a7cf050`) and the end-to-end rows (`6ebc636`: R4-E3, R4-E6, the new R4-E6c, R4-E7, and R4-E6b for R2). On `r4b`: the further-reading form's wording (`60110df`, cherry-picked onto `r4r` as `da343d2`). On `r4r`: the references guard reads the stored list's addresses and attributes outside its words, where a label may name a host as text (`f1a85a0`).
- `[NOTE]` For R2 (from 16 November, when `src/app/actions/lesson.ts` is Release 4's): `saveLessonNarrative` asks `sanitizeNarrativeBlocks(blocks, "reflection-intro")` for a reflection lesson (the standard reflection, or a lesson of a course's own whose main content is a reflection), so an introduction over the limit is refused as "The introduction is too long — at most 10,000 characters. Shorten it.", as its editor's problem line says; until then a save names it "Block 1 (Paragraph)" with a block's advice. `narrative.ts`'s part is in (U4); R4-E6b holds the end-to-end test (fixme until R2).

- `[BUILD]` Reading link text (D104, §3.2), a fifth Foundation-light commit on the shared contracts (`6c38b61`: `link-text.ts`, U2, and the hostile table with seven more misleading labels, five more accepted labels and eight more misleading titles), made on `r4` and cherry-picked onto `r4b` (`a48b7ab`) and `r4r` (`9b64301`) the same day. One leading "www." is removed before a host's site is told, so "GOV.UK" over www.gov.uk and "www.gov.uk" over gov.uk show the link's site. A name after two slashes is a host ("//paypal.com", "ftp://paypal.com"), and so is one after a slash at a word's start ("Login: /paypal.com"), while a name inside a path still belongs to it ("docs/report.de"); a colon followed by no digit starts no port ("evil.example:paypal.com" shows paypal.com). A raised or lowered character, a circled one, a sign of letters or a modifier letter ends a name ("paypal.com¹", "PayPal.com™", "paypal.comʼs"), and a hair space shows nothing. A name is read as it shows in Latin letters: in a name with a Latin letter, or of Cyrillic look-alikes only, every label of Latin letters and Cyrillic or Greek look-alikes, with marks of no letter of their own dropped ("pаypal.com" with a Cyrillic а over its own punycode shows paypal.com; "paypal.соm.evil.example"; "paypal.co̲m"), while Greek words stay Greek ("Αθήνα.Το", "προσπάθεια.να"). A label that starts with a top-level domain and a hyphen shows that name in front of the link's site ("paypal.com-login.evil.example"). A title prefix makes an abbreviation at a name's start or after words of one or two letters ("Co.KG", "o.Univ.Prof."), never when it and the labels after it make a public suffix ("Amazon.Co.UK" is a host), and a title-case "Prof" or "Ltd" after words of at most four letters ends one ("Jun.Prof.", "Pty.Ltd."). Over a web link, an email address's first part that starts with "www." or ends in a country's or a generic top-level domain (com, net, org, edu, gov, mil, int, info, biz) is a host ("paypal.com@evil.example"); a person's name isn't ("anna.schmidt@uni.example"). On `r4`: the sanitizer reads a link with its word through a hair space and names the link's text with it (`bbb2c5b`, U3), and R4-E6, R4-E6c and R4-E7 carry the new rows (`7b11981`).

A character that may stand apart from a name (a raised, lowered or circled one, a Roman numeral, a sign of letters) is read both as a space and as part of the name, and only as a space where it ends a name ("paypal.com¹", "PayPal.com™"); as part of a name, one that shows a letter is that letter and a raised digit or a sign of letters is nothing, so "paypal.cᵒm", "paypal.ⓒⓞⓜ", "paypaⅼ.com", "PayPal™.com" and "paypal.com¹.evil.example" show paypal.com. A mark that follows no letter or digit belongs to none and is read as nothing ("paypal.̲com", "̲paypal.com", "www.paypal.̲com/login"). Cyrillic г and п are read as r and n ("micгosoft.com" over its own punycode shows microsoft.com). A word in lower case after a title is an abbreviation's, whatever the public suffixes hold ("Dr.in Maria Huber", "Mag.a Dr.in Huber"), and a title before a public suffix in title case or capitals is a host ("Co.UK", "EE.Co.UK"). A public suffix of more than one label named by itself ("gov.gr", "GOV.UK") shows every site under it ("Gov.gr Wallet" over wallet.gov.gr, "Find a course on GOV.UK" over nationalcareers.service.gov.uk), and an address of its own host is that host ("https://www.gov.gr", "www.gov.uk", "gov.uk/guidance"). A name or an address is read in time proportional to its length: a public suffix is looked for over at most its longest rule's labels (`MOST_SUFFIX_LABELS`), and punctuation, dots and hyphens at a name's end are trimmed from the end, once. On `r4`: the sanitizer reads each text of a cluster once (`9d9017e`: U3, `sanitize-cost.test.ts`, and the Link form's and paste's tests), and R4-E6, R4-E6c and R4-E7 carry the new rows (`e6c5010`). On `r4b` and `r4r`: a further-reading title is read, once cleaned, as link text is (`0d54e7c`, `3f51a2f`).

A title and its feminine ending "in", as Austrian usage writes them, raised or not, is an abbreviation ("Dir.in Mag.a Huber", "Dir.ⁱⁿ Mag.ª Huber", "Bgm.ⁱⁿ", "OStR.ⁱⁿ", "HR.ⁱⁿ Dr.ⁱⁿ"; `TITLES_WITH_ENDING`: Abg, BEd, Bgm, DDr, DI, Dir, Doz, GR, HR, LAbg, OStR, StR, Vzbgm, in their written case), while "Amazon.in", "dir.in", "HR.com" and "Doz.at/login" are hosts. A name's last label that starts with a top-level domain in lower case and a hyphen shows the name that ends there ("paypal.com-login", "PayPal.com-Login", "amazon.de-bestellung"; "Booking.com-Konto" over booking.com shows its link's site), and one in capitals there shows none ("WEB.DE-Postfach", "1.IT-Sicherheit"). The browser and the server read link text alike: a name the URL parser refuses that ends in a top-level domain is read as written ("xn--a.paypal.com", whose punycode label Node's parser refuses and Chrome's keeps, shows paypal.com in both), and a character that Unicode 16 (Node 22's) doesn't assign is read as a space and as nothing in both (`unassigned.ts`: 728 ranges, 4.4 KB, generated by `scripts/unassigned-code-points.ts` from Node 22.19 on 22 October 2026; `unassigned.test.ts` checks it against the runtime while its Unicode is 16.0; regenerate when the server's Node moves to a newer Unicode), so "paypal.᫐com" and "paypal.co𖿴m", of Unicode 17's characters, show paypal.com in Chrome 155 as on the server. On `r4`: U3, U4, the Link form's and paste's tests read the same texts (`b785469`), and R4-E6, R4-E6c, R4-E7 and R4-E8 carry the new rows (`969774e`). On `r4b` and `r4r`: a further-reading title is read, once cleaned, as link text is (`fc426c6`, `a950f3b`).

The phishing probes' 327 texts (1,635 with a sentence, "www.", a path and capitals): none newly accepted, 85 newly refused. Node 22 and Chrome 155 on the 2,472 texts of the code-and-robustness check, each over five links (the link text answer and the save's): 301 differed before, none after, on `r4`, `r4b` and `r4r`. - `[BUILD]` A ninth Foundation-light commit on the shared contracts (`55201bc`: `link-host.ts`, `link-url.ts`, `link-text.ts`, U1, U2 and the new `link-cost.test.ts`), made on `r4` and cherry-picked onto `r4b` (`e2029a4`) and `r4r` (`8acbe38`) the same day, one patch on all three. D103: a web address's host is one DNS can hold (RFC 1035): as DNS writes it (in punycode), no label of more than 63 characters and at most 253 in all; a longer one is refused as a host the address can't use ("This address has no full site name (like example.org), or one that can't be used."), or as an address with a user name when it holds one. A host longer than that as typed, counting only the letters and digits that stay its own whatever the URL parser makes of it (`beyondDns`), is refused before the address is parsed, so every address is checked in time in proportion to its length; the address completion of the Link form, paste and the further-reading form (`toLinkInput`) leaves such an address as typed, for the check to refuse. D104: link text that shows a name with a label longer than DNS allows is read as the URL parser reads it, without parsing that label whole: the label is checked in pieces (its start, its end, the letters and digits that decide how the parser checks it, and 32 more at a time) and stands for itself in the name's hostname; in a host longer than DNS allows, which no quick link reads, an ideographic full stop is a dot to the reading of "https:" and "http:" addresses, as it is to the URL parser.
- `[NOTE]` For the owner: the time a check takes on Node 22.19 (median of 20 runs after a warm-up), before (`f5702c8`) and after: a 2,030-character international address, 13 to 14 ms and 0.02 to 0.07 ms; link text of 10,000 characters holding one international label, in six shapes, 139 to 147 ms and 4.1 to 5.2 ms (8.5 to 31.6 times the time of eighty texts of 125 characters, and 0.7 to 0.8 times); the design review's 1 MB lessons (`lesson-1mb.mts`), 2.39 s and 0.01 s a save for blocks of long international hrefs, 5.75 s and 0.16 s for blocks of long international link text; a 1 MB lesson of both in turns (12 blocks), 2.5 to 2.7 s and 36 to 51 ms. Ordinary writing and the phishing probes compared before and after, every answer (the link text's, and the save's issues, problems and stored text): round 1's 134 phrases, the 706 rows and sentences of the writing checks, the 115 phrases of the third round's extra files, the probes' 327 texts with their variants (1,635) and the 82 benign texts with raised characters: identical, on `r4`, `r4b` and `r4r`. Addresses refused that were accepted: those whose host DNS can't hold, among them an international label longer than 63 characters in punycode (from about 21 to 28 CJK ideographs, 50 Greek letters, 57 German letters with umlauts). The link-text rule's redesign (a visible site label, a smaller rule) remains the owner's decision; this commit changes how fast the rule decides.

- `[DECISION]` The site label (the owner's decision of 22 October 2026, "Yes, show the site"; D104, D105): every named web link in lesson text (paragraphs, key messages, reflection introductions, text pieces, and the references list) shows the site it opens in a small box after its words, inside the link: a ", " that screen readers hear, then the site, so the link is heard as "the guide, example.org (opens in a new tab)", its site before what it opens; still no icon (D105). The site is the one D104 compares: the host without one leading "www.", taken to its registrable domain (the host itself for an IP address and under it, jp, no, pl and us), in ASCII, an international name in punycode for Release 4. The box is left out only when the whole of the link's words is its address (with or without its own scheme, one leading "www." and one trailing slash, the host in any letter case); email links and quick links show none. Links to our own file store, which stay links on render (D103), show their site like any other. The lesson editor's block preview shows the box as learners see it.
- `[BUILD]` A tenth Foundation-light commit on the shared contracts (`139e895`: the new `link-site.ts`, with `linkSite` and `textIsSite`, its test, and the box's look in `globals.css`), made on `r4`, to be cherry-picked onto `r4b` and `r4r` in step 4, with their own labels. Part A on `r4`: `RichTextView` draws the box after a named link's words, live and in the inert preview, through `QuickLink`'s children (`1cb01d0`; `QuickLink.tsx` unchanged), and R4-E15 (`e47c872`). The box: small, muted on its own background, rounded, in a monospace face; a line breaks before it, never inside it unless the site alone is longer than the line; `unicode-bidi: isolate` and `direction: ltr`; rules for forced colours, the platform's high contrast, the dyslexia font and print; `text-indent: 0`, so the references list's hanging indent never moves its text. The ", " is clipped where it stands (`.seq-link-sep`), not `.sr-only`: an absolutely positioned element counts as a block in a link's name, and browsers then read "the guide , example.org".
- `[NOTE]` For step 4 (spec 4.5, 5.4), the other named links learners get: further reading (`r4b`, `LessonFurtherReading.tsx`) draws each title as a link with the host on a line under it, outside the link; its title link takes the same box (`linkSite`, `textIsSite` and the classes of the shared commit), so the site is heard with the link and shown as in lesson text, and the line under it shows the same site. The references list (`r4r`) is drawn by `RichTextView`: its named links take the box with Part A, with no change on `r4r`. The course page's read view and the link manager are staff screens.

- `[DECISION]` D104 and D105 as amended by the owner's decision of 22 October 2026 ("Yes, show the site"; DECISIONS.md): the box after each named link's words is what tells learners where it goes, and D104 refuses link text only where it shows, by its look, an address of another site: a name after a scheme or after two slashes at a word's start, a "www." name of three labels or more, a name a path, query, fragment or port follows, an email address, or a name of Latin letters ending in a common top-level domain in lower case (46: com, net, org, edu, gov, int, info, biz, io, app, eu and the countries of the partners and their neighbours, not pl); a user name before a host after a scheme, or a label mixing Latin with Greek or Cyrillic letters, shows somewhere else whatever the link opens. Text is read as learners see it (NFC, without what shows nothing or a combining mark left over, the ideographic, fullwidth and halfwidth ideographic full stops read as dots) and once more in NFKC, where a sign NFKC makes a word of ("™") is nothing. A name is read in the letters of ASCII, Latin-1, Latin Extended-A and -B, Greek and Cyrillic, by code point, so the browser and the server read the same characters as a name's; a name inside a path, or in an address's path, query or fragment, belongs to it; a last label that starts with a top-level domain and a hyphen ends the name ("www.ams.at-Seite"). Sites are compared as before; a common top-level domain in front of a host's site shows that name, in an email address's domain too. A name in capitals or title case, one ending in another top-level domain, a file's name, a title and an abbreviation show no address: such link text keeps its link, and its box shows where it goes. The editor, the Link form, paste and a save name every misleading text alike: it "shows a different address than the link opens".
- `[BUILD]` An eleventh Foundation-light commit on the shared contracts (`1f7f6d3`: `link-text.ts`, `link-host.ts`, `link-url.ts`, `top-level-domains.ts`, `scripts/public-suffixes.ts`, `package.json` and `pnpm-lock.yaml`, U1, U2, `link-cost.test.ts`, the new `link-text.corpus.test.ts` with its corpus, `link-text-answers.test.ts` and `link-cost-symbols.test.ts`, and the hostile table), made on `r4`, to be cherry-picked onto `r4b` and `r4r` after the tenth in step 4 (it applies to both cleanly; checked without changing either worktree). Removed: the look-alike table and the three readings per text; the modifier-letter, mark-of-no-letter and hair-space readings; `unassigned.ts` with its generator and test; the title, title-ending and Austrian title lists; the list of generic top-level domains; the email first-part rule; the rule for a top-level domain and a hyphen in front of a site; the URL-parser fallback; the quick-link reading of link text; `namesOnly` and "looks like a web address". Kept: `top-level-domains.ts`, `link-host.ts`, `checkLinkHref` and the touching-links reading. D103: `beyondDns` counts every character of a host between its full stops (letters, digits, marks, symbols and punctuation alike), so a host of symbols, punctuation or marks that DNS can't hold is refused before the address is parsed; in link text a label longer than DNS allows stands for itself and is never written in punycode. `TOP_LEVEL_DOMAINS` is the generator's output, which `top-level-domains.test.ts` checks; tldts-icann is a development dependency pinned to 7.4.2, and `package.json`'s engines name Node 22. On `r4`: the sanitizer and the Link form name every misleading text alike, and the words a link is read with end at any whitespace (`8d846f7`: U3, `sanitize-cost.test.ts`, the Link form's, the problem line's and U4's tests); R4-E3, R4-E6, R4-E6c, R4-E7 and R4-E8 follow the rule, and R4-E6c and R4-E7 save and draw a file's name and a name in capitals as links with their site (`1e6cbcf`). The hostile table holds the labels and titles D104 refuses; the corpus test holds the 909 honest texts with their answers and the refused phishing probes.

- `[BUILD]` A thirteenth Foundation-light commit on the shared contracts (`ab32b65` on `r4`, cherry-picked onto `r4b` as `4339753` and `r4r` as `44743be`; one patch on all three): `linkSite` gives an email link's site, its address's domain as D104 compares it (`asciiDomain`, now exported from `link-text.ts`) taken to its registrable domain ("support@paypal.com.evil.example" shows evil.example), and `textIsSite` leaves its box out only when its words, as a whole, are its bare address, with or without "mailto:", in printable ASCII; `linkWords` draws a named link's words with each run of blank characters (whitespace, the braille blank, the Hangul fillers, the interlinear annotation marks, with whatever shows nothing among them, across the link's parts) as one space, one space, one no-break space or one narrow no-break space kept as typed; `globals.css` gives every link in lesson text, outside the editor, a generated left-to-right mark just inside each end, never copied, stored or heard (an empty alternative text), so no right-to-left character around or in a link moves the link, its words or its box among a line's other links, or splits a link across lines around another; `checkLinkHref` refuses an email address whose domain DNS can't hold as typed, every character counted, before reading it. U1 and the box's tests follow (`link-url.test.ts`, `link-site.test.ts`). One blob on all three: `link-site.ts` `a00c732`, `link-url.ts` `f3e6b64`, `link-text.ts` `96b8344`, `link-site.test.ts` `ddb36f7`, `link-url.test.ts` `b98e487`. On `r4`: `RichTextView` draws a named link's words through `linkWords`, live and in the inert block preview, and an email link with its box (`6d29a82`); R4-E15 hears an email link's site, and the new R4-E15b keeps each link's box beside its words at 320, 640 and 1,280 px (`add6f2f`). On `r4b`, cherry-picked onto `r4r` (`30ec117`; `2710697`): a further-reading title is drawn through `linkWords` (U18).

In system Chrome 155, on 13 hostile paragraphs (right-to-left marks, Arabic letter marks and right-to-left punctuation around links, one of them wrapping; Hebrew words; runs of spaces, em spaces, braille blanks, Hangul fillers, zero-width spaces and an imitation of a box) at 143 widths from 300 to 1,294 px: each link's own box is the first box after its words begin, beside their last visible character or on the next line, with no gap of more than a line in its words; the link-rules review's own probe passes at 320, 640 and 1,280 px. Further reading's titles, each a flex item laid out on its own, hold the same at 101 widths on `r4b`. The characters Chrome draws with width but no ink, in the system's fonts, over every assigned code point: whitespace, U+2800, U+3164, U+FFA0 and U+FFF9–U+FFFB, every one in the class `linkWords` draws as one space. Node 22.19 against Chrome 155 over every code point: `linkWords` and `textIsSite` answer alike, and the email box alike wherever both engines accept the address; the 4,699 code points where they differ are all ones Node doesn't assign, which Node's address check refuses.

Four shared commits, made on `r4` and cherry-picked onto `r4b` and `r4r`, one patch on all three: `30ab82c` (`ef861da`, `0cda6a9`): a cost test measures CPU time (`process.cpuUsage()`), the long and the short work in turn, each at the least of nine rounds (`cpu-time.test-helpers.ts`, new), and `link-text.test.ts` reads each of its twelve 10,000-character shapes in less than 3 times eight of 1,250 and in under 10 ms; `5642d2c` (`7d0c334`, `06e80f2`): `showsAnotherAddressOverAny` (new) compares what a text shows with all the links it is read over at once, each address checked once and each site looked up once, at a cost that follows the addresses shown and the links, never their product; `showsAnotherAddress` is it over one link, and `addressesShown` finds the names in front of a host's site once a text; every answer is the rule's as before (`link-text.test.ts`: the corpus's 1,472 texts and the rule's own cases over each of 38 addresses and over 24 groups of two to five of them, against the rule's comparison pair by pair; `link-cost-links.test.ts`, new); `d46fe81` (`d2e3007`, `d9fce2f`): `checkLinkHref` writes a web address as the URL standard does in every engine, a path's "^" and "|" as typed and its "%5E" and "%7C" as "^" and "|" (`standardPath`, new), and counts its 2,048 characters in that form, and `textIsSite` compares a path in that form, so the server and the learner's browser accept the same addresses, with the same site and box (`link-site.test.ts`, through a URL parser that writes a path as Chrome does); `de4f0a9` (`b25b15a`, `9df6ca2`): `r0-links` types an author's address once the paragraph's text box has the cursor. On `r4`: the sanitizer compares each reading of a paragraph's links with all its links at once, checks an address once a text and reads our file store's host once a text (`2596b5a`; `sanitize-cost.test.ts`: every touching-links case looks up at most one site a character of its paragraph; 440 touching links to two addresses of one site, each three names of another host of it, in less than 3 times the CPU time of the same text as one link; 330 touching links, each to its own host, in less than 3 times the same links apart; two 1 MB saves of 100 paragraphs of five touching links in under 0.5 s; the long names and addresses in the new form, in under 10 ms); R4-E3 sends its next keys once Apply has put the cursor back in the text (`9077431`). On `r4b`, cherry-picked onto `r4r` (`a3765a3`; `fcc4a3c`): the add action stores an address with "^" or "|" in its path as the standard writes it (`lesson-reading.test.ts`), and the new R4-E9b sees a 2,041-character one titled "PayPal – sign in" with "evil.example" in its box in the learner's Chrome, through the branch's own `link-site.ts` and `link-url.ts` (the list's place on the learner's screens comes with R2). - `[NOTE]` Known limits after the link-rules adversarial round (verdict §7; from here on the rule and the box change only under its stop rule):
  - The Link form unwraps Google's redirect in the form the spec names, `www.google.com/url?q=` (and google.com); another form of it, or one on a country's Google domain, is checked as the address it is; the partners' clipboards of 9 November show whether they use one repeatedly.
  - Paste keeps the words of Word's tracked changes and comments as Word puts them on the clipboard (paste, not the rule or the box); the captures of 9 November confirm it.
  - A link's words are drawn as typed: a blank at their end stays inside the link, and the box follows it, beside the next word (the box is shown and right).
  - The box shows a link's site also where its words already name it (verdict §1).
  - The box follows a link's words, also inside a word the link covers part of ("Europass [europa.eu]-Lebenslauf"); the partner guide says to link whole words.
  - Two links joined by "/" with no blank between them are read as one address, as touching links are.
  - Words that show a brand's, a short link's or a renamed site's address over a link to another registrable domain are refused (D104); the clipboards show how often partners write them.
  - An APA reference whose DOI is linked to the publisher's page is refused, its words showing doi.org; the partner guide says to link the DOI.
  - A no-break hyphen or an en dash ends a name: "wahl‑o‑mat.de" is read from its last part, mat.de.
  - An email link that can't be kept is named with the address check's message (D103).
  - Paste drops a link on an image without a note (paste, not the rule or the box).
  - International names are shown in the box in punycode (the owner's item 13; verdict §6.5).
  - The box is drawn for learners and in the inert preview; the editable text an author types in draws none.
  - Direction marks and zero-width characters in a link's words stay in them; the box is isolated from them.
  - Letters of Unicode 17 that Node 22 doesn't assign are refused by the server's check, which decides; the browser's may accept them (within verdict §7).
  - The Link form completes an address typed or pasted into it of up to about 4 million characters; past that, the form stops, in the author's own tab only (not the rule or the box).
  - The editor puts the cursor in a paragraph a frame after it opens (Tiptap's focus), and the tests wait for it; taking it at once is the owner's call.

- `[DECISION]` **The link rule and the site box are frozen** (2026-10-23; the link-rules review's plan, step 6, and its stop rule). The rule (D104 as narrowed, rule G), the box (D105 as amended) and the address check (D103) are these blobs, one on `r4` (`d3d372b`), `r4b` (`a3765a3`) and `r4r` (`fcc4a3c`) alike: `link-text.ts` `d052f64`, `link-site.ts` `a270085`, `link-url.ts` `ff9e778`, `top-level-domains.ts` `b738589`, and the corpus test `link-text.corpus.test.ts` `0a4d55e` with its corpus `link-text.corpus.json` `5093324`; `link-host.ts`, which they import, is `2ab2988` on all three. Release 4's security review of 17–18 November reviews these blobs. **Stop rule:** from here on the rule or the box changes only for a missing, wrong, hidden or imitable box; an accepted address with a scheme, "www." or a path, or a host in lower case ending in a common top-level domain, over another site; a check over its budget (10 ms a text, 0.5 s a 1 MB save); a false refusal in the partners' real clipboards (due 9 November) or the hands-on paste (16–18 November) at more than 1 link in 50, or for a service partners use repeatedly; or a must-fix of the security review. Look-alike or capitalised words over another site, with the box shown, are known limits (above) and are never patched. A change under the stop rule names its ground and records its new blobs in this log.
- `[VERIFY]` The plan's targets on the frozen blobs, measured with copies of the link-rules review's scripts against `r4` at `d3d372b` (Node 22.19.0, Chrome 155.0.8059.85; load 9 to 14, a Release 3 build running):
  - **Honest text:** of the 909 honest text-and-link pairs, 19 refused (at most 20), each showing another site than its link opens; of the app's messages and the guides (31,796 words), 11 words refused, each showing an address.
  - **Hostile text:** of the phishing probes' 1,635 variants (327 texts, each alone, in a sentence, with "www.", with a path and in capitals), 995 refused (219 of the 327 texts; at least 995); with real top-level domains, 1,008 (223).
  - **The box:** every hostile variant the rule accepts and the address check takes is drawn with the box naming the site it opens, 575 of 575 (562 of 562 with real top-level domains; 65 more have an address the check refuses, and are drawn as no link). The look-alike tail of the first design review: 308 of 932 refused, and 624 of 624 accepted drawn with the right box. The second design review's confusables sweep (its 1,065 texts and 187 one-letter look-alikes of "paypal.com", over five links: 6,260 pairs): 2,141 refused, and of the 3,309 links drawn every one has the box naming its site but the 8 whose whole words are their own address, where the box is left out by design (4 pairs, a text of one control character, draw no link). Each checked by script through the sanitizer and `RichTextView`, against tldts-icann's registrable domain.
  - **Speed:** link text of 10,000 characters at most 3.6 ms over the design reviews' worst inputs, and 0.5 to 1.6 ms over the length guards' inputs; a host of 2,017 characters refused in 0.05 ms; a 1 MB save at most 0.05 s for the design reviews' lessons, and 0.10 to 0.18 s of CPU time for 100 paragraphs of touching links.
  - **Node against Chrome:** 4,803 code points answer differently in the two engines, all but one unassigned in Node's Unicode 16; over the review's 58,701 texts and five links, 5 texts answer differently, each holding U+A7F1, which Node doesn't assign, and in each Chrome refuses where Node accepts (17 answers), never the other way; the box answers alike on every text, and the corpus test's 1,472 pairs alike; the address check's 309 addresses (every printable ASCII character in a path, a query and a fragment, sixteen percent escapes, the two 2,041-character addresses) answer alike in check, site and box. The same on `r4`, `r4b` and `r4r`.

- `[FIX]` **A pasted text reads as its review accepted** (link round 5, W2; paste cleanup, not the rule or the box): tracked deletions are left out with their text — `<del>` (Word desktop on Windows and Mac writes each in a span of class `msoDel`), Word for the web's deleted run (class `TrackChangeTextDeletion`) and `<w:del>`; tracked insertions are kept as text (`<ins>` and `<w:ins>` unwrapped); Word's comments are left out — the reference after the commented words ("[MT1]": `msocomanchor`, `#_msocom_…`, `MsoCommentReference`, the comment mark) and the comments after the text (`mso-element:comment-list`), the commented words kept. LibreOffice copies without deleted text and writes a comment as an HTML comment, which the editor never reads. A paragraph whose mark a reviewer deleted stays a paragraph of its own. First in `paste-cleanup.ts` (`paste-review.ts`), so on `r4` a pasted link's words are the accepted ones: a link whose words a reviewer changed is kept with the new words, and no note says it wasn't. The clipboards are hand-built after Word's and Word for the web's markup and marked so (`e2e/fixtures/paste/review-marks.ts`); the partners' clipboards of 9 November join or replace them. `r4` `3372e63` (and `aaa825b`, the kept links), `r4b` `3b3f5d8`, `r4r` `49ef9d8`.
- `[FIX]` **Blanks at either end of a link's words are kept in the text, outside the link** (link round 5, W3; `r4` `ee3bb43`): a blank is whitespace (a space, a no-break space, a tab; `link-blanks.ts`). On paste a kept link's blanks go just before and after it; the Link form links words selected with the space after them, or typed with blanks around them, without the blanks; the stored format moves every kept link's blanks out of it after the paragraph's links are read (`sanitize.ts`), as text with the same bold and italic, never deleted — so every save, editor opening and learner page has them outside, older texts too. What the link rule refuses or keeps is the same, and a stored text read again keeps every link it kept. The site's box follows the words; the next word is parted from it by the blank. `sanitize-cost.test.ts`'s touching links with a space are 400 a paragraph (two nodes a link under the size limit); `RichTextView.test.ts` draws whitespace at the end of a link's words after the link, and blank characters that aren't whitespace (braille blank, Hangul filler) as one space in the words, as before. The rule and the box are unchanged.
- `[FIX]` **"Start a new course" keeps what an editor types before the page's scripts have run, and opens the created course's page as a page load of its own** (`r4` `46fb38a` and `16e911d`, `r4b` `d18b457` and `596f332`, `r4r` `c08b952` and `fdb888d`): the page, not only the test, measured on `r4r`'s build. The form reads its fields once as the page takes over, so a title typed before then enables "Create draft" and stays when the next field is typed in (React keeps what was typed on screen without telling the form). The course page opens with `window.location.assign`, at the address the router used, so no server action of "Create courses" still on its way can take the navigation back (Next's router applies such an action's page after a later navigation, vercel/next.js#88343: `r4r`'s failures of "an editor creates a course" were this, the form back at /en/content, empty, the course created). "an editor creates a course" (`db-backed-progress.spec.ts`) types the title while the page's scripts are held, waits for "Create draft", then the rest.

- `[FIX]` **Only Word's own review marks are left out of a paste** (link round 5, W2's rule for `<del>` and for a comment's style; `paste-review.ts`, one patch: `r4` `a01b24b`, `r4b` `1e29508`, `r4r` `0ed63de`): a `<del>` goes with its text as Word's tracked deletion when Word's marks show it: the class `msoDel` (its own or a span's around it), the author as Word writes it (`cite="mailto:…"`), the time as Word writes it (`datetime` to the minute, "2026-10-20T09:14"), or a page Word wrote (its generator, program id or namespace; Word writes struck-through text as `<s>`). Any other `<del>` keeps its words, which the editor reads as plain text, as before W2: a web page's or Markdown's strikethrough (GitHub, Obsidian and Typora draw it as `<del>`), so "Bring ~~a laptop~~ a notebook." arrives whole. Word's comment list (`mso-element:comment-list`) and each comment in it (`mso-element:comment`) go whole; a paragraph in a comment's style outside them (`MsoCommentText`, `MsoCommentSubject`, `msocomtxt`) is text and stays, so text copied out of a Word comment pastes as text. The rest of W2 is unchanged: tracked insertions kept as text; the comment's reference and Word's comment mark, Word for the web's deleted run and `<w:del>` left out; LibreOffice's clipboard as it comes. `paste-review.test.ts` runs the whole paste cleanup in jsdom (a development dependency pinned to 29.1.1, the Vitest environment of that file only; nothing of it reaches the app): the three review fixtures read as accepted, and what an author pasted keeps its words. `pnpm-lock.yaml` adds jsdom and its dependencies; lighthouse's trace engine asks for `legacy-javascript` "latest", which resolves to 0.0.3.

- `[MERGE]` **Release 3 with Release 2's course save rules merged into Release 4** (2026-10-23): `r3` at `b97963c` (the commit, not the branch's moving head), 15 commits since `d6a39cf`: the verification's follow-ups (`d197eee`, `cb20cac`, `736f994`), `r2` merged twice (`152e7bb`, `r2` at `491e5a5`; `a630a94`, `r2` at `828e647`), `ac2656a` and their build-log entries; merged into `r4` (`e3f996c`), `r4b` (`edd6fdb`) and `r4r` (`3b909d6`). It brings: what a course save sends isn't known on the server until that save is confirmed, so an answer changed back while its save is on its way is saved (D62 as built); a page hidden or left with a course save on its way, or one that failed, keeps what the server hasn't confirmed in the tab's outbox (D68 as built); a Comp Card save that fails once the page has changed since it was sent is followed by another (D80 as built); the clean-up's dry run and run move the focus to what they show (WCAG 2.4.3); a "[" anywhere in a web address's host but at its start means the text isn't a quick link, and a host that starts with "[" is measured to the next "]" (D55).   - `src/lib/rich-text/linkify.ts` (`r4`): Release 3's reading of a web host (`webHost`: as the URL parser finds it, none when a "[" stands anywhere in it but at its start) and the length guard run first, before anything parses the address; a typed address is then a quick link exactly when the one address check takes it (D103), as at the first merge. The file's comment holds both rules. On `r4b` and `r4r` the file merged without a conflict: Release 3's host reading beside `isEmailAddress`, the URL parser after the guard, as on `r3`; the two files are identical, and the guard's code (`webHost`, `hostFits`) is the same on the three branches.

- `[BUILD]` **The course page's further-reading hints say what learners get** (spec 4.6; `r2-review.md` finding 1): `58be20d` on `r4b`.
  - "Further reading alone doesn't make a lesson appear for learners." is said only of a lesson learners don't get because nothing in it can be shown yet (the plan's `notLive: "empty"`). Any other lesson they don't get (hidden, or the Release 2 player switched off) says "Learners don't get this lesson now, so they don't see its further reading." A lesson they get says neither.
  - "Every language gets this lesson: its only material is for German learners, …" is said of a lesson learners get only where the player's fallback shows its documents to the other languages, which happens only while the lesson shows nothing else in their language. The page reads that from its own view of the lesson in each language: the in-page preview of a lesson drawn piece by piece (`byLang[l].sectioned`), else the stage learners get (`byLang[l].stage`). The standard reflection lesson always shows its introduction and questions, so its German-only document reaches German learners only, and the card no longer promises it to the others.
  - One rule for the fallback, `lessonDocumentsFor` in `src/lib/cms/item-language.ts`, with `stageShowsOwnContent` and `sectionShowsOwnContent`. Three places use it: `learner-course.ts`'s stage lessons, `sections/resolve.ts`'s lessons drawn piece by piece, and the course page's hints (`readingHints` and `linkHints` in `LessonLinkManager.tsx`, called by `LessonSection.tsx`). What learners get is unchanged.
